Viṣṇu Purāṇa

Book 2, Chapter 8

Description of the sun: his chariot; its two axles: his horses. The cities of the regents of the cardinal points. The sun's course: nature of his rays: his path along the ecliptic. Length of day and night. Divisions of time: equinoxes and solstices, months, years, the cyclical Yuga, or age of five years. Northern and southern declinations. Saints on the Lokáloka mountain. Celestial paths of the Pitris, gods, Vishńu. Origin of Gangá, and separation, on the top of Meru, into four great rivers.

VP 2.8.1

प्राशर उवाच व्याख्यातमैतद्ब्रह्मण्डसंस्थानं तव सुव्रत । ततः प्रमाणसंस्थाने सूर्यादीनां शृणुष्व मै

Sri Paraasharar - This entire Brahmaanda, its form, location, expanse and measure, were told to you by me. Listen to me about the location and size, measure of the Sun, various planets.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

prāśara uvāca vyākhyātamaitadbrahmaṇḍasaṃsthānaṃ tava suvrata — Sri Paraasharar - This entire Brahmaanda, its form, location, expanse and measure, were told to you by me. Listen to me about the location and size, measure of the Sun, various planets.; tataḥ pramāṇasaṃsthāne sūryādīnāṃ śṛṇuṣva mai — Sri Paraasharar - This entire Brahmaanda, its form, location, expanse and measure, were told to you by me. Listen to me about the location and size, measure of the Sun, various planets.

Śrīdhara

vivṛtaṃ vistareṇedaṃ jyotiścakraṃ vicakṣaṇaiḥ | mayā tu svīyabodhāya saṅkṣepeṇoparvaṇyate — Atha jyotischakram | Vyaakhyaatam iti | Etat brahmaanda samsthaanam mayaa tava vyaakhyaatam | The nakshatra mandala is going to be told. The Brahmaanda samsthaana was told to you by me. First, the Sun's chariot is explained in great detail. How the movements happen, the various parts of the Sun's chariot are told in great detail.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

prāśara uvāca vyākhyātamaitadbrahmaṇḍasaṃsthānaṃ tava suvrata | tataḥ pramāṇasaṃsthāne sūryādīnāṃ śṛṇuṣva mai ||1||

vivṛtaṃ vistareṇedaṃ jyotiścakraṃ vicakṣaṇaiḥ | mayā tu svīyabodhāya saṅkṣepeṇoparvaṇyate

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

विवृतं विस्तरेणेदं ज्योतिश्चक्रं विचक्षणैः । मया तु स्वीयबोधाय सङ्क्षेपेणोपर्वण्यते

Śrīdhara commentary.

Atha jyotischakram | Vyaakhyaatam iti | Etat brahmaanda samsthaanam mayaa tava vyaakhyaatam | The nakshatra mandala is going to be told. The Brahmaanda samsthaana was told to you by me. First, the Sun's chariot is explained in great detail. How the movements happen, the various parts of the Sun's chariot are told in great detail.

VP 2.8.2

योजनानां सहस्राणि भास्करस्य रथो नव । ईषादण्डस्तथैवास्य द्विगुणो मुनिसत्तम

The Sun's ratha is nine thousand yojanaas. The pole is twice that. The chariot has various parts. The body of the chariot, which is supported by two axles, these axles have wheels. At the centre of the axle is the main pole which extends forward. There is the yoke, to which are tied the horses, which are controlled by two reins. The wheel has got hub, spokes, outer circumference.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yojanānāṃ sahasrāṇi bhāskarasya ratho nava — The Sun's ratha is nine thousand yojanaas. The pole is twice that. The chariot has various parts. The body of the chariot, which is supported by two axles, these axles have wheels. At the centre of the axle is the main pole which extends forward. There is the yoke, to which are tied the horses, which are controlled by two reins. The wheel has got hub, spokes, outer circumference.; īṣādaṇḍastathaivāsya dviguṇo munisattama — The Sun's ratha is nine thousand yojanaas. The pole is twice that. The chariot has various parts. The body of the chariot, which is supported by two axles, these axles have wheels. At the centre of the axle is the main pole which extends forward. There is the yoke, to which are tied the horses, which are controlled by two reins. The wheel has got hub, spokes, outer circumference.

Śrīdhara

īṣādaṇḍaḥ akṣayugayoḥ sandhānārtho daṇḍaḥdviguṇaḥ aṣṭādaśayojanasahasraḥ — Yojanaanaam iti | Bhaaskarasya deha vyaapto ratha bhaago nava saahasrah; vakshyamaana gandharvaadi parivaara avakaashaartho rathaamshah cha sahasra maatrah; ittham dasha sahasro rathah; Mandalam bhaaskarasya atha yojanaanaan nibodhata | Nava yojana saahasro vistaaro bhaaskarasya tu | iti Dasha yojana saahasro vistaaraayaamato rathah iti cha tejomayatayaa doorato mandalatvena drushyamaanasya tat dehasya nava sahasratayaa tat rathasya cha dasha sahasratayaa vaayu ukteh | Eeshaadandah dvigunah - vimshati yojana sahasrah | [[108]] The main body of the Sun is 9000 yojanaas. There is also a part of the chariot where all the gandharvaas and others are supported; this portion of the chariot is 1000 yojanaas. So, the total length of the chariot is 10000 yojanaas. Vaayu Puraana says that Surya Mandala is 9000 yojanaas. The expanse of the chariot is 10000 yojanaas. What is seen as the bright body of the Sun is 9000 yojanaas, and the chariot is 10000 yojanaas. The pole is twice this, 20000 yojanaas.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yojanānāṃ sahasrāṇi bhāskarasya ratho nava | īṣādaṇḍastathaivāsya dviguṇo munisattama ||2||

īṣādaṇḍaḥ akṣayugayoḥ sandhānārtho daṇḍaḥdviguṇaḥ aṣṭādaśayojanasahasraḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ईषादण्डः अक्षयुगयोः सन्धानार्थो दण्डःद्विगुणः अष्टादशयोजनसहस्रः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Yojanaanaam iti | Bhaaskarasya deha vyaapto ratha bhaago nava saahasrah; vakshyamaana gandharvaadi parivaara avakaashaartho rathaamshah cha sahasra maatrah; ittham dasha sahasro rathah; Mandalam bhaaskarasya atha yojanaanaan nibodhata | Nava yojana saahasro vistaaro bhaaskarasya tu | iti Dasha yojana saahasro vistaaraayaamato rathah iti cha tejomayatayaa doorato mandalatvena drushyamaanasya tat dehasya nava sahasratayaa tat rathasya cha dasha sahasratayaa vaayu ukteh | Eeshaadandah dvigunah - vimshati yojana sahasrah | [[108]] The main body of the Sun is 9000 yojanaas. There is also a part of the chariot where all the gandharvaas and others are supported; this portion of the chariot is 1000 yojanaas. So, the total length of the chariot is 10000 yojanaas. Vaayu Puraana says that Surya Mandala is 9000 yojanaas. The expanse of the chariot is 10000 yojanaas. What is seen as the bright body of the Sun is 9000 yojanaas, and the chariot is 10000 yojanaas. The pole is twice this, 20000 yojanaas.

VP 2.8.3

सार्धकोटिस्तथा सप्त नियुतान्यधिकानि वै । योजनानां तु तस्याक्षस्तत्र चक्रं प्रतिष्ठितम्

One crore and fifty seven lakh yojanaas, is the axle, at the end of the axle are the wheels established.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

sārdhakoṭistathā sapta niyutānyadhikāni vai — One crore and fifty seven lakh yojanaas, is the axle, at the end of the axle are the wheels established.; yojanānāṃ tu tasyākṣastatra cakraṃ pratiṣṭhitam — One crore and fifty seven lakh yojanaas, is the axle, at the end of the axle are the wheels established.

Śrīdhara

sārddhakoṭir iti — the phrase sārdhakoṭi indicates the measure; meru-mānasottarācalayor yāvad-antam — as far as the distance between Meru and Mānasottara; tāvadevākṣasya mānam — that is the measure of the axle; tasyākṣo meru-mūrdhani kṛtaḥ — its axle is fixed at the summit of Meru; mānasottare kṛtetara-bhāgaḥ — its other part reaches Mānasottara; yatra protaṃ ravi-ratha-cakram — where the wheel of the Sun’s chariot is fixed; taila-yantravat paribhramati — it revolves like the wheel of an oil-press; iti śukokteḥ — thus Śuka has said

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

sārdhakoṭistathā sapta niyutānyadhikāni vai | yojanānāṃ tu tasyākṣastatra cakraṃ pratiṣṭhitam ||3||

sārddhakoṭir iti-merumānasottarācalayor yāvadantaṃ tāvadevākṣasya mānam | tasyākṣo meromūrddhani kṛto mānasottare kṛtetarabhāgo yatra protaṃ ravirathacakraṃ tailayantravanmāsottaragirau paribhramati | iti śukokteḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

सार्द्धकोटिर् इति-मेरुमानसोत्तराचलयोर् यावदन्तं तावदेवाक्षस्य मानम् । तस्याक्षो मेरोमूर्द्धनि कृतो मानसोत्तरे कृतेतरभागो यत्र प्रोतं रविरथचक्रं तैलयन्त्रवन्मासोत्तरगिरौ परिभ्रमति । इति शुकोक्तेः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains that the solar wheel is fixed to the axle, whose two parts extend between Meru and Mānasottara. Dhruva governs the motion through the wind-reins, so the wheel, axle, and Dhruva revolve together.

VP 2.8.4

त्रिनाभिमति पञ्चारे षण्णेमिन्यत्रयात्मके । संवत्सरमये कृत्स्त्रं कालचक्रं प्रतिष्ठितम्

hundred thousand leagues long ; on which is fixed a wheel with three naves, five spokes, and

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

trinābhimati pañcāre ṣaṇṇeminyatrayātmake — hundred thousand leagues long; saṃvatsaramaye kṛtstraṃ kālacakraṃ pratiṣṭhitam — on which is fixed a wheel with three naves, five spokes, and

Śrīdhara

cakram upāsanārthaṃ viśinaṣṭi — he specifies the wheel for meditation; tri-nābhi — having three hubs; tri-mekhala — having three rims; pañca-āra — having five spokes; ṣaṭ ṛtavaḥ nemayaḥ — the six seasons are its rims; saṃvatsaramayam kāla-cakram — the wheel of time is formed as a year; tasmin jyotiṣ-cakraṃ pratiṣṭhitam — the celestial wheel is established in it

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

trinābhimati pañcāre ṣaṇṇeminyatrayātmake | saṃvatsaramaye kṛtstraṃ kālacakraṃ pratiṣṭhitam ||4||

cakramupāsanārthaṃ viśinaṣṭi–trinābhimati iti | nābhiḥ dhruvaḥ | yatrākṣaḥ protaḥ sa ca trimekhalaḥ | ahnaḥ evātra nābhitvenopāsyatvāt mekhalārūpāstisro nābhayaḥ pūrvāhṇa madhyāhnāparāhṇastadyukte pañcāre pañca saṃvatsaraparivatsarādayaḥ arāḥ śalākā yasmin | paṇṇemini ṣaṭ ṛtavo nemayaḥ prāntabalayāni tadvati | kālātmakatvāt akṣayātmake saṃvatsaramaye saṃvatsaravikāratvāt saṃvatsaratvenopāsyatvācca tanmayatvam | taduktaṃ mātsye— ahastrinābhisūryasya ekacakrasya vai smṛtam | arāḥ saṃvatsarāḥ pañca nemyaḥ ṣaḍ ṛtavaḥ smṛtāḥ iti | tasmin kālacakraṃ kālopamānabhūtaṃ kṛtsnaṃ jyotiścakraṃ pratiṣṭhitam āśritam, sūryagatyadhīnatvāt kālacakrapaṭalasya

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

चक्रमुपासनार्थं विशिनष्टि–त्रिनाभिमति इति । नाभिः ध्रुवः । यत्राक्षः प्रोतः स च त्रिमेखलः । अह्नः एवात्र नाभित्वेनोपास्यत्वात् मेखलारूपास्तिस्रो नाभयः पूर्वाह्ण मध्याह्नापराह्णस्तद्युक्ते पञ्चारे पञ्च संवत्सरपरिवत्सरादयः अराः शलाका यस्मिन् । पण्णेमिनि षट् ऋतवो नेमयः प्रान्तबलयानि तद्वति । कालात्मकत्वात् अक्षयात्मके संवत्सरमये संवत्सरविकारत्वात् संवत्सरत्वेनोपास्यत्वाच्च तन्मयत्वम् । तदुक्तं मात्स्ये— अहस्त्रिनाभिसूर्यस्य एकचक्रस्य वै स्मृतम् । अराः संवत्सराः पञ्च नेम्यः षड् ऋतवः स्मृताः इति । तस्मिन् कालचक्रं कालोपमानभूतं कृत्स्नं ज्योतिश्चक्रं प्रतिष्ठितम् आश्रितम्, सूर्यगत्यधीनत्वात् कालचक्रपटलस्य

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: cakramupāsanārthaṃ viśinaṣṭi–trinābhimati iti | nābhiḥ dhruvaḥ | yatrākṣaḥ protaḥ sa ca trimekhalaḥ | ahnaḥ evātra nābhitvenopāsyatvāt mekhalārūpāstisro nābhayaḥ pūrvāhṇa madhyāhnāparāhṇastadyukte pañcāre pañca saṃvatsaraparivatsarādayaḥ arāḥ śalākā yasmin | paṇṇemini ṣaṭ ṛtavo nemayaḥ prāntabalayāni tadvati | kālātmakatvāt akṣayātmake saṃvatsaramaye saṃvatsaravikāratvāt saṃvatsaratvenopāsyatvācca tanmayatvam | taduktaṃ mātsye— ahastrinābhisūryasya ekacakrasya vai smṛtam | arāḥ saṃvatsarāḥ pañca nemyaḥ ṣaḍ ṛtavaḥ smṛtāḥ iti | tasmin kālacakraṃ kālopamānabhūtaṃ kṛtsnaṃ jyotiścakraṃ pratiṣṭhitam āśritam, sūryagatyadhīnatvāt kālacakrapaṭalasya

VP 2.8.5

हयाश्च सप्तच्छदांसि तेषां नामानि मे शृणु । गायत्री च बृहत्युष्णिग्जगती त्रिष्टुभेव च । अनुष्टुप्पङ्क्तिरित्युक्ता छन्दांसि हरयो रवेः

The seven horses tied to the ratha are said to be the seven cchandas - Gayatri, Bruhati, Ushnik, Jagati, Trishtup, Anushthup, Pankti. These are for meditation purposes, as explained in the Commentary.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

hayāśca saptacchadāṃsi teṣāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu — The seven horses tied to the ratha are said to be the seven cchandas - Gayatri; gāyatrī ca bṛhatyuṣṇigjagatī triṣṭubheva ca — Bruhati; anuṣṭuppaṅktirityuktā chandāṃsi harayo raveḥ — Ushnik

Śrīdhara

akṣāntaram āha catvāriṃśat — he states the distance between the axles: forty; sārdha-pañca-catvāriṃśat-sahasra-pramāṇaḥ — measuring forty-five and a half thousand; tatra trayī-ātmanaḥ sūryasya — there, of the Sun whose nature is the three Vedas; gāyatrī-ādi-chando-rūpeṇa upāsyān hayān āha — he states the horses to be worshipped in the form of meters beginning with Gāyatrī; paṅktiḥ iti etāni chandāṃsi — “Paṅkti,” and these are the meters; raveḥ harayaḥ hayāḥ uktāḥ — the rays of the Sun are called horses

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

hayāśca saptacchadāṃsi teṣāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu | gāyatrī ca bṛhatyuṣṇigjagatī triṣṭubheva ca | anuṣṭuppaṅktirityuktā chandāṃsi harayo raveḥ ||5||

akṣāntaram āha—catvāriṃśad iti | sārddhapañcacatvāriṃśatsahasrapramāṇa ityarthaḥ ॥ tatra trayyātmanaḥ sūryasya gāyatryādicchandorūpeṇopāsyān hayānāha hayāś ceti | paṅktirityetāni chandāṃsi raverharayo hayā uktā ityanvayaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अक्षान्तरम् आह—चत्वारिंशद् इति । सार्द्धपञ्चचत्वारिंशत्सहस्रप्रमाण इत्यर्थः ॥ तत्र त्रय्यात्मनः सूर्यस्य गायत्र्यादिच्छन्दोरूपेणोपास्यान् हयानाह हयाश् चेति । पङ्क्तिरित्येतानि छन्दांसि रवेर्हरयो हया उक्ता इत्यन्वयः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: akṣāntaram āha—catvāriṃśad iti | sārddhapañcacatvāriṃśatsahasrapramāṇa ityarthaḥ ॥ tatra trayyātmanaḥ sūryasya gāyatryādicchandorūpeṇopāsyān hayānāha hayāś ceti | paṅktirityetāni chandāṃsi raverharayo hayā uktā ityanvayaḥ

VP 2.8.6

चत्वारिंशत्सहस्राणि द्वितीयोऽक्षो विवस्वतः । पञ्चान्यानि तु सार्धानि स्यन्दनस्य महामते

The Sun's second axle is 45500 yojanaas in length.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

catvāriṃśatsahasrāṇi dvitīyo'kṣo vivasvataḥ — The Sun's second axle is 45500 yojanaas in length.; pañcānyāni tu sārdhāni syandanasya mahāmate — The Sun's second axle is 45500 yojanaas in length.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

catvāriṃśatsahasrāṇi dvitīyo'kṣo vivasvataḥ | pañcānyāni tu sārdhāni syandanasya mahāmate ||6||

Śrīdhara commentary.

No commentary.

VP 2.8.7

आक्षप्रमाणमुभयोः प्रमाणं तद्युगार्धयो । ह्रस्वोक्षस्तद्युगार्धेन ध्रुवाधारो रथस्य वै । द्वितीयेक्षे तु तच्चक्रं सस्थितं मानसाचले

The yoke is also of the same length as the smaller axle. One half of the yoke and one end of the smaller axle at the northern end are tied to the reins made of Vaayu, and held by Dhruva. To the big axle, the wheel is attached, and it is located at the Manasottara parvata.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ākṣapramāṇamubhayoḥ pramāṇaṃ tadyugārdhayo — The yoke is also of the same length as the smaller axle. One half of the yoke and one end of the smaller axle at the northern end are tied to the reins made of Vaayu, and held by Dhruva. To the big axle, the wheel is attached, and it is located at the Manasottara parvata.; hrasvokṣastadyugārdhena dhruvādhāro rathasya vai — The yoke is also of the same length as the smaller axle. One half of the yoke and one end of the smaller axle at the northern end are tied to the reins made of Vaayu, and held by Dhruva. To the big axle, the wheel is attached, and it is located at the Manasottara parvata.; dvitīyekṣe tu taccakraṃ sasthitaṃ mānasācale — The yoke is also of the same length as the smaller axle. One half of the yoke and one end of the smaller axle at the northern end are tied to the reins made of Vaayu, and held by Dhruva. To the big axle, the wheel is attached, and it is located at the Manasottara parvata.

Śrīdhara

mahākṣa-pramāṇaṃ yuge’py ati-diśati — the measure of the great axle is also applied to the yoke; īṣāgre tiryaṅ nibaddhaḥ aśva-yojanārtho daṇḍo yugam — the yoke is the crossbar fixed across the front of the pole for harnessing the horses; akṣasya yat pramāṇam — whatever is the measure of the axle; tad eva yugārdhayoḥ saṃhatya pramāṇam — that same measure, taken together with the two halves of the yoke, is its measure; hrasvākṣaḥ ... dhruvādhāraḥ — the smaller axle is connected with the yoke and supported by Dhruva; pravāhādi-vāyu-vaśāt jyotiścakraṃ paribhramati — through the force of the winds beginning with Pravāha, the celestial wheel revolves; evaṃ rathaprakriyā — such is the arrangement of the chariot

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ākṣapramāṇamubhayoḥ pramāṇaṃ tadyugārdhayo | hrasvokṣastadyugārdhena dhruvādhāro rathasya vai | dvitīyekṣe tu taccakraṃ sasthitaṃ mānasācale ||7||

mahākṣapramāṇaṃ yuge'pyatidiśati-akṣa iti | īṣāgre tiryaṅ nibaddho'śvayojanārtho daṇḍo yugam | akṣasya yat pramāṇaṃ tad eva yugārddhayoḥ saṃhatya pramāṇam | yugasya pramāṇam iti ca vaktavye yugārddhayor ityuktam | tatrāyaṃ bhāvaḥ-yasmin ubhayato'śvā badhyante tadyugamīṣāgrayuktamadhyasthānādubhayataḥ samaṃ bhavati | atra tvekata eva saptāśvāḥ, na tūbhayataḥ, asaṅgaisturagairyukte yataścakraṃ tataḥ sthitaiḥ iti liṅgokteḥ | ato'śvayukte bhāge yugasya dairghyam anyato hrasvatvam iti śaṅkā mā bhūdityetadartha yugārddhayoḥ samayoḥ saṃhatya tatpramāṇamityuktam | yuktañcākṣayugayor ekapramāṇatvam iti | hrasvokṣasyopayogaṃ darśayannāha—hrasvo'kṣa iti | sārddhapañcacatvāriṃśatsahasro hrasvākṣastasya rathasya yugārddhena saha vāyuraśminibaddho dhruvādhāro'sti | tathā śukaḥ—tasminnakṣekṛtamūlo dvitīyo'kṣastailayantrākṣavad dhruve kṛtoparibhāgaḥ iti-mātsye coktam | yugākṣakoṭisthau tasya dakṣiṇe syandanasya tu | dhruveṇa ca gṛhītau tau raśmī yau nayato ravim ॥ iti nirantaroktahrasvāṅkapekṣayā dvitīye tu mahākṣe tathā cātra rathaprakriyā-bhūtalād yojanalakṣe sūryaḥ, pañcadaśalakṣe dhruvaḥ, caturaśītiyojanasahasrocchrayo meruḥ, arddhalakṣocchrāyo mānasottarācalaḥ, taduparyarddhalakṣocchrayo meroścopari ṣoḍaśayojanasahasrocchraye vāyuskandhe sārddhasaptakṣottarasārddhakoṭipramāṇo ravirathasyākṣo mānasītaraprāntaprautaikacakrastiryag vitato'sti | tarmiścākṣe cakraprānte nibaddhabhūlo hrasvo'kṣo vāyuraśmiyukto dhruvādhāro'sti | yugañca dakṣiṇārdhe vāyuraśmisambaddhaṃ dhruvādhāraṃ tathaivāsti | pravāhādivāyuvaśācca dvādaśarāśyātmakaṃ jyotiścakraṃ pratyahaṃ meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvat paribhramati | tadvegavaśācca sūryaḥ pratyahaṃ meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvannapi svagatyā rāśyabhimukham api kiñcit kiñcit pracalati | tatra dakṣiṇāyanādiṣu dhruvagṛhītavāyupāśaprasāraṇākarṣaṇābhyāṃ ravergativaicitryād yathāyathamahorātrāṇi hrasvadīrghasamāni bhavanti | evaṃ candrādīnām api jyotiścakravaśāt pratyahaṃ meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvatām api nakṣatrābhimukhī svakīyā gatiranyaiva aśvinyāṃ dṛṣṭānāṃ bharaṇyādau darśanāt | etacca darśanāt | etacca tatra ślokavyākhyāne sphuṭībhaviṣyati

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

महाक्षप्रमाणं युगेऽप्यतिदिशति-अक्ष इति । ईषाग्रे तिर्यङ् निबद्धोऽश्वयोजनार्थो दण्डो युगम् । अक्षस्य यत् प्रमाणं तद् एव युगार्द्धयोः संहत्य प्रमाणम् । युगस्य प्रमाणम् इति च वक्तव्ये युगार्द्धयोर् इत्युक्तम् । तत्रायं भावः-यस्मिन् उभयतोऽश्वा बध्यन्ते तद्युगमीषाग्रयुक्तमध्यस्थानादुभयतः समं भवति । अत्र त्वेकत एव सप्ताश्वाः, न तूभयतः, असङ्गैस्तुरगैर्युक्ते यतश्चक्रं ततः स्थितैः इति लिङ्गोक्तेः । अतोऽश्वयुक्ते भागे युगस्य दैर्घ्यम् अन्यतो ह्रस्वत्वम् इति शङ्का मा भूदित्येतदर्थ युगार्द्धयोः समयोः संहत्य तत्प्रमाणमित्युक्तम् । युक्तञ्चाक्षयुगयोर् एकप्रमाणत्वम् इति । ह्रस्वोक्षस्योपयोगं दर्शयन्नाह—ह्रस्वोऽक्ष इति । सार्द्धपञ्चचत्वारिंशत्सहस्रो ह्रस्वाक्षस्तस्य रथस्य युगार्द्धेन सह वायुरश्मिनिबद्धो ध्रुवाधारोऽस्ति । तथा शुकः—तस्मिन्नक्षेकृतमूलो द्वितीयोऽक्षस्तैलयन्त्राक्षवद् ध्रुवे कृतोपरिभागः इति-मात्स्ये चोक्तम् । युगाक्षकोटिस्थौ तस्य दक्षिणे स्यन्दनस्य तु । ध्रुवेण च गृहीतौ तौ रश्मी यौ नयतो रविम् ॥ इति निरन्तरोक्तह्रस्वाङ्कपेक्षया द्वितीये तु महाक्षे तथा चात्र रथप्रक्रिया-भूतलाद् योजनलक्षे सूर्यः, पञ्चदशलक्षे ध्रुवः, चतुरशीतियोजनसहस्रोच्छ्रयो मेरुः, अर्द्धलक्षोच्छ्रायो मानसोत्तराचलः, तदुपर्यर्द्धलक्षोच्छ्रयो मेरोश्चोपरि षोडशयोजनसहस्रोच्छ्रये वायुस्कन्धे सार्द्धसप्तक्षोत्तरसार्द्धकोटिप्रमाणो रविरथस्याक्षो मानसीतरप्रान्तप्रौतैकचक्रस्तिर्यग् विततोऽस्ति । तर्मिश्चाक्षे चक्रप्रान्ते निबद्धभूलो ह्रस्वोऽक्षो वायुरश्मियुक्तो ध्रुवाधारोऽस्ति । युगञ्च दक्षिणार्धे वायुरश्मिसम्बद्धं ध्रुवाधारं तथैवास्ति । प्रवाहादिवायुवशाच्च द्वादशराश्यात्मकं ज्योतिश्चक्रं प्रत्यहं मेरुं प्रदक्षिणीकुर्वत् परिभ्रमति । तद्वेगवशाच्च सूर्यः प्रत्यहं मेरुं प्रदक्षिणीकुर्वन्नपि स्वगत्या राश्यभिमुखम् अपि किञ्चित् किञ्चित् प्रचलति । तत्र दक्षिणायनादिषु ध्रुवगृहीतवायुपाशप्रसारणाकर्षणाभ्यां रवेर्गतिवैचित्र्याद् यथायथमहोरात्राणि ह्रस्वदीर्घसमानि भवन्ति । एवं चन्द्रादीनाम् अपि ज्योतिश्चक्रवशात् प्रत्यहं मेरुं प्रदक्षिणीकुर्वताम् अपि नक्षत्राभिमुखी स्वकीया गतिरन्यैव अश्विन्यां दृष्टानां भरण्यादौ दर्शनात् । एतच्च दर्शनात् । एतच्च तत्र श्लोकव्याख्याने स्फुटीभविष्यति

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains the yoke, the great and small axles, and their connection with Dhruva. The celestial wheel revolves under the force of the cosmic winds, while the Sun moves through the zodiac.

VP 2.8.8

मानसोत्तरशैलस्य पूर्वतो वासवी पुरी । दक्षिणे तु यम स्यान्याप्रतीच्यां वरुणस्य च । उत्तरेण च सोमस्य तासां नामानि मे शृणु

the Mánasa mountain . The seven horses of the sun's car are the metres of the Vedas, Gáyatrí, Vrihatí, Ushńih, Jayatí, Trisht́ubh, Anusht́ubh, and Pankti. The city of

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

mānasottaraśailasya pūrvato vāsavī purī — the Mánasa mountain . The seven horses of the sun's car are the metres of the Vedas; dakṣiṇe tu yama syānyāpratīcyāṃ varuṇasya ca — Gáyatrí; uttareṇa ca somasya tāsāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu — Vrihatí

Śrīdhara

meruḥ catur-diśam udaya-astamaya-ādi-upalakṣaṇa-bhūtam pura-catuṣṭayam āha — he states the four cities marking the directions of rising and setting around Meru; mānasottara iti — “Manasottara”; pūrvataḥ ity ādi bhārata-varṣa-sthāna-apekṣayā ucyate — “east” and so on are stated relative to Bhārata-varṣa; pūrvādi-vibhāgasya sūrya-darśana-ādi-apekṣatve — when the divisions beginning with east depend on seeing the Sun and so on; na aniyamasya vakṣyamāṇatvāt — because a lack of fixedness will be described; yāmyā yamasya purī — Yāmyā is the city of Yama

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

mānasottaraśailasya pūrvato vāsavī purī | dakṣiṇe tu yama syānyāpratīcyāṃ varuṇasya ca | uttareṇa ca somasya tāsāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu ||8||

meroścaturdiśamudayāstamayādyupalakṣaṇabhūtaṃ puracatuṣṭayam āha—mānasottara iti | pūrvata ityādi bhāratavarṣasthānāpekṣayocyate | pūrvādivibhāgasya sūryadarśanādyapekṣatve nāniyamasya vakṣyamāṇatvāt | yāmyā yamasya purī

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

मेरोश्चतुर्दिशमुदयास्तमयाद्युपलक्षणभूतं पुरचतुष्टयम् आह—मानसोत्तर इति । पूर्वत इत्यादि भारतवर्षस्थानापेक्षयोच्यते । पूर्वादिविभागस्य सूर्यदर्शनाद्यपेक्षत्वे नानियमस्य वक्ष्यमाणत्वात् । याम्या यमस्य पुरी

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: meroścaturdiśamudayāstamayādyupalakṣaṇabhūtaṃ puracatuṣṭayam āha—mānasottara iti | pūrvata ityādi bhāratavarṣasthānāpekṣayocyate | pūrvādivibhāgasya sūryadarśanādyapekṣatve nāniyamasya vakṣyamāṇatvāt | yāmyā yamasya purī

VP 2.8.9

वस्वौकसारा शक्रस्य याम्या संयमनी तथा । पुरी सुखाजलेशस्य सोमस्य च विभावरी

The names of these cities are: Indra's city is called Vasvoukasaaraa. Yama's city is called Samyamanee. Varuna's city is called Sukhaa. Soma's city is called Vibhaavaree.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

vasvaukasārā śakrasya yāmyā saṃyamanī tathā — The names of these cities are: Indra's city is called Vasvoukasaaraa. Yama's city is called Samyamanee. Varuna's city is called Sukhaa. Soma's city is called Vibhaavaree.; purī sukhājaleśasya somasya ca vibhāvarī — The names of these cities are: Indra's city is called Vasvoukasaaraa. Yama's city is called Samyamanee. Varuna's city is called Sukhaa. Soma's city is called Vibhaavaree.

Śrīdhara

meruḥ catur-diśam udaya-astamaya-ādi-upalakṣaṇa-bhūtam pura-catuṣṭayam āha — he states the four cities marking the directions of rising and setting around Meru; mānasottara iti — “Manasottara”; pūrvataḥ ity ādi bhārata-varṣa-sthāna-apekṣayā ucyate — “east” and so on are stated relative to Bhārata-varṣa; pūrvādi-vibhāgasya sūrya-darśana-ādi-apekṣatve — when the divisions beginning with east depend on seeing the Sun and so on; na aniyamasya vakṣyamāṇatvāt — because a lack of fixedness will be described; yāmyā yamasya purī — Yāmyā is the city of Yama

meroścaturdiśamudayāstamayādyupalakṣaṇabhūtaṃ puracatuṣṭayam āha—mānasottara iti | pūrvata ityādi bhāratavarṣasthānāpekṣayocyate | pūrvādivibhāgasya sūryadarśanādyapekṣatve nāniyamasya vakṣyamāṇatvāt | yāmyā yamasya purī

VP 2.8.10

काष्ठां गतो दक्षिणतः क्षिप्तेषुरिव सर्पति । मैत्रेय भगवान्भानुर्ज्योतिषां चक्रसंयुतः

The Sun moves like an arrow thrown from a bow. Along with the jyotischakra, graha mandala, he moves as though thrown like an arrow from a bow, towards the south.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kāṣṭhāṃ gato dakṣiṇataḥ kṣipteṣuriva sarpati — The Sun moves like an arrow thrown from a bow. Along with the jyotischakra, graha mandala, he moves as though thrown like an arrow from a bow, towards the south.; maitreya bhagavānbhānurjyotiṣāṃ cakrasaṃyutaḥ — The Sun moves like an arrow thrown from a bow. Along with the jyotischakra, graha mandala, he moves as though thrown like an arrow from a bow, towards the south.

Śrīdhara

tatra tāvat aindrīm avadhim kṛtvā — first taking the city of Indra as the limit; sūryasya udaya-astamana-ādikam vakṣyan — while describing the Sun's rising and setting; uttarāyaṇa-ante atyantam uttarāṃ diśam gataḥ — having gone far to the north at the end of the northern course; dakṣiṇataḥ parāvṛttim āha — he describes the turning back toward the south; kāṣṭhām gataḥ dakṣiṇāyanam praviṣṭaḥ — having reached the direction and entered the southern course; kṣiptaḥ iṣuḥ yathā — like an arrow shot; tathā śīghram gacchati — he travels swiftly

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kāṣṭhāṃ gato dakṣiṇataḥ kṣipteṣuriva sarpati | maitreya bhagavānbhānurjyotiṣāṃ cakrasaṃyutaḥ ||10||

tatra tāvadaindrīmavadhiṃ kṛtvā sūryasya udayāstamanādikaṃ vakṣyan uttarāyaṇānte'tyantamuttarāṃ diśaṃ gatasya dakṣiṇataḥ parāvṛttim āha—kāṣṭhām iti | dakṣiṇato yā kāṣṭhā tāṃ gataḥ dakṣiṇāyanaṃ praviṣṭaḥ san kṣipta iṣuryathā tathā śīghraṃ gacchati

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत्र तावदैन्द्रीमवधिं कृत्वा सूर्यस्य उदयास्तमनादिकं वक्ष्यन् उत्तरायणान्तेऽत्यन्तमुत्तरां दिशं गतस्य दक्षिणतः परावृत्तिम् आह—काष्ठाम् इति । दक्षिणतो या काष्ठा तां गतः दक्षिणायनं प्रविष्टः सन् क्षिप्त इषुर्यथा तथा शीघ्रं गच्छति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Kaashthaam gata iti | Atha tasya uparishthaat tu bhramato dakshinaayanam iti lainga ukteh indraadi pura upari gagane bhraman dakshina kaashthaam gato jyotischakra praantam gatvaa sheeghram (bhramati) | Kulaala chakra praantastho jantuh iva | When Sun is moving in front of Indra's city, in the front, on top, moving in the sky, when he reaches the southern tip, just like the wheel of a potter, when an insect is moving along the wheel, just like this, the Sun is moving.

VP 2.8.11

अहोरात्रव्यवस्थानकारणं भगवान्रविः । देवयानः परः पन्था योगिनां क्लेशसंक्षये

Sun is only the cause of establishing the day and night. He is also the ultimate path for yogis, which is called Devayaana, when they get rid of all the karmaas.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ahorātravyavasthānakāraṇaṃ bhagavānraviḥ — Sun is only the cause of establishing the day and night. He is also the ultimate path for yogis, which is called Devayaana, when they get rid of all the karmaas.; devayānaḥ paraḥ panthā yogināṃ kleśasaṃkṣaye — Sun is only the cause of establishing the day and night. He is also the ultimate path for yogis, which is called Devayaana, when they get rid of all the karmaas.

Śrīdhara

tataś ca aindrī-prabhṛti tāsu purīṣu — then, in those cities beginning with Indra’s; saṃsāriṇām ahorātra-vyavasthāna-kāraṇaṃ raviḥ bhavati — the Sun becomes the cause of day and night for embodied beings; kleśānāṃ rāgādīnāṃ saṃkṣaye sati — when afflictions such as attachment have ceased; yogināṃ kramamukti-bhājāṃ devayānaḥ paraḥ panthāḥ — for yogins attaining gradual liberation, the Devayāna is the supreme path; apunarāvṛttikaraḥ sa eva — it is that path which causes non-return; sūrya-dvāreṇa te virajāḥ prayānti — through the solar gateway they proceed, free from impurity; iti śruteḥ — thus says the scripture

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ahorātravyavasthānakāraṇaṃ bhagavānraviḥ | devayānaḥ paraḥ panthā yogināṃ kleśasaṃkṣaye ||11||

tataścaindrīprabhṛti tāsu purīṣu saṃsāriṇām ahorātravyavasthānakāraṇaṃ ravirbhavati | kleśānāṃ rāgādīnāṃ saṅkṣaye sati yogināṃ kramamuktibhājāṃ devayānaḥ paraḥ śreṣṭhaḥ apunarāvṛttikaraḥ panthāśca sa eva bhavati | sūryadvāreṇa te virajāḥ prayānti, sa yāvadicchen manastāvadādityaṃ gacchati | ityādiśruteḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ततश्चैन्द्रीप्रभृति तासु पुरीषु संसारिणाम् अहोरात्रव्यवस्थानकारणं रविर्भवति । क्लेशानां रागादीनां सङ्क्षये सति योगिनां क्रममुक्तिभाजां देवयानः परः श्रेष्ठः अपुनरावृत्तिकरः पन्थाश्च स एव भवति । सूर्यद्वारेण ते विरजाः प्रयान्ति, स यावदिच्छेन् मनस्तावदादित्यं गच्छति । इत्यादिश्रुतेः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The Sun establishes day and night in the cities of the gods. For yogins whose afflictions have ended, the Devayāna is the supreme path to gradual liberation and non-return.

VP 2.8.12

दिवसस्य वरिर्मध्ये सर्वाकालं व्यवस्थितः । सर्वद्वीपेषु मैत्रेय निशार्धस्य च सन्मुखः

During the day, he is located all the time in the centre. Even in midnight, he is located like this only, in all the dveepaas. Depending on whether he is seen or unseen in that particular place, it becomes day and night.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

divasasya varirmadhye sarvākālaṃ vyavasthitaḥ — During the day, he is located all the time in the centre. Even in midnight, he is located like this only, in all the dveepaas. Depending on whether he is seen or unseen in that particular place, it becomes day and night.; sarvadvīpeṣu maitreya niśārdhasya ca sanmukhaḥ — During the day, he is located all the time in the centre. Even in midnight, he is located like this only, in all the dveepaas. Depending on whether he is seen or unseen in that particular place, it becomes day and night.

Śrīdhara

yena prakāreṇa ahorātryādi-vyavasthāna-kāraṇam raviḥ — the manner in which the Sun causes the arrangement of day and night; madhyāhne lakṣa-yojana-ucchrite vyomni — in the sky at midday, raised a hundred thousand yojanas; tīvra-sphuṭa-śukla-ātapena pratapan — shining with intense, clear, white heat; sarva-dvīpeṣu — in all the islands; dūratva-nibandhanāt mandātapatvam — the weakness of the heat is due to distance

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

divasasya varirmadhye sarvākālaṃ vyavasthitaḥ | sarvadvīpeṣu maitreya niśārdhasya ca sanmukhaḥ ||12||

yena prakāreṇa ahorātryādivyavasthākāraṇaṃ ravistadāha divasasya iti pañcabhiḥ | yathāsmin dvīpe bhāratavarṣe madhyāhne lakṣayojanocchrite vyomni tīvrasphuṭaśuklātapena raviḥ pratapan vartate, tathā sarvakālam udayāstamayādiṣvapi sarvadvīpeṣvapi nīcairbhūlagnavaddarśanasya mandātapatvādīnāñca dūratvādinibandhanatvāt | yathāha—vāyuḥ vidūrabhāvāccārkasya prodyatasya viraśmitā raktatā ca, viraśmitvād raktatvāccāpyanuṣṇatā | iti | yadā yasmin dvīpavarṣādau madhyāhne vartate, tadā tatsamānasūtrasya dvīpāntarādiṣu jāyamānasya niśārddhasya arddharātrasya sammukho bhavati

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

येन प्रकारेण अहोरात्र्यादिव्यवस्थाकारणं रविस्तदाह दिवसस्य इति पञ्चभिः । यथास्मिन् द्वीपे भारतवर्षे मध्याह्ने लक्षयोजनोच्छ्रिते व्योम्नि तीव्रस्फुटशुक्लातपेन रविः प्रतपन् वर्तते, तथा सर्वकालम् उदयास्तमयादिष्वपि सर्वद्वीपेष्वपि नीचैर्भूलग्नवद्दर्शनस्य मन्दातपत्वादीनाञ्च दूरत्वादिनिबन्धनत्वात् । यथाह—वायुः विदूरभावाच्चार्कस्य प्रोद्यतस्य विरश्मिता रक्तता च, विरश्मित्वाद् रक्तत्वाच्चाप्यनुष्णता । इति । यदा यस्मिन् द्वीपवर्षादौ मध्याह्ने वर्तते, तदा तत्समानसूत्रस्य द्वीपान्तरादिषु जायमानस्य निशार्द्धस्य अर्द्धरात्रस्य सम्मुखो भवति

Śrīdhara commentary.

The in between directions, north-east, south-east, etc., are called vidiks.

VP 2.8.13

उदयास्तमने चैव सर्वकालं तु संमुखे । विदिशासु त्वशेषासु तथा ब्रह्मन् दिशासु च

the sages who have overcome the inflictions of the world. Whilst the sun, who is the discriminator of all hours, shines in

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

udayāstamane caiva sarvakālaṃ tu saṃmukhe — the sages who have overcome the inflictions of the world. Whilst the sun, who is the discriminator of all hours, shines in; vidiśāsu tvaśeṣāsu tathā brahman diśāsu ca — the sages who have overcome the inflictions of the world. Whilst the sun, who is the discriminator of all hours, shines in

Śrīdhara

udayāstamane sarvakālaṃ bhavataḥ | te ca parasparaṃ sammukhe raveḥ samāna sūtrasthe bhavataḥ — Śrīdhara gloss: udayāstamane sarvakālaṃ bhavataḥ | te ca parasparaṃ sammukhe raveḥ samāna sūtrasthe bhavataḥ

udayāstamane caiva sarvakālaṃ tu saṃmukhe | vidiśāsu tvaśeṣāsu tathā brahman diśāsu ca ||13||

udayāstamane sarvakālaṃ bhavataḥ | te ca parasparaṃ sammukhe raveḥ samāna sūtrasthe bhavataḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

उदयास्तमने सर्वकालं भवतः । ते च परस्परं सम्मुखे रवेः समान सूत्रस्थे भवतः

Śrīdhara gloss: udayāstamane sarvakālaṃ bhavataḥ | te ca parasparaṃ sammukhe raveḥ samāna sūtrasthe bhavataḥ

VP 2.8.14

यैर्यत्र दृश्यते भास्वान्स तेषामुदयः स्मृतः । तिरोभावं च यत्रैति तत्रैवास्तमनं रवेः

Whoever sees the Sun there this is said as as rising. Who does not see there, it is said as setting.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yairyatra dṛśyate bhāsvānsa teṣāmudayaḥ smṛtaḥ — Whoever sees the Sun there this is said as as rising. Who does not see there, it is said as setting.; tirobhāvaṃ ca yatraiti tatraivāstamanaṃ raveḥ — Whoever sees the Sun there this is said as as rising. Who does not see there, it is said as setting.

Śrīdhara

tatra hetum āha yaiḥ yatra — here he states the reason: by whom and where; sukhau tu udaya-astamayau — rising and setting are pleasant; sarvadā madhya-ahne ekaḥ sataḥ arkasya na asti — there is no setting for the one Sun when it is always at midday

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yairyatra dṛśyate bhāsvānsa teṣāmudayaḥ smṛtaḥ | tirobhāvaṃ ca yatraiti tatraivāstamanaṃ raveḥ ||14||

tatra hetum āha—yairyatra iti | sukhau tu udayāstamayau | sarvadā madhyāhne eka sato'rkasya nāsta eva

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत्र हेतुम् आह—यैर्यत्र इति । सुखौ तु उदयास्तमयौ । सर्वदा मध्याह्ने एक सतोऽर्कस्य नास्त एव

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: tatra hetum āha—yairyatra iti | sukhau tu udayāstamayau | sarvadā madhyāhne eka sato'rkasya nāsta eva

VP 2.8.15

नैवास्तमनमर्कस्य नोदयः सर्वदा सतः । उदयास्तमनाख्यं हि दर्शनादर्शनं रवेः

[[122]] Darshana and adarshana are said to be the rising and setting of the Sun, whether they see the Sun, or not see the Sun. In reality, Sun does not rise or set as what we perceive here. It is by people seeing or not seeing that such things happen.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

naivāstamanamarkasya nodayaḥ sarvadā sataḥ — [[122]] Darshana and adarshana are said to be the rising and setting of the Sun, whether they see the Sun, or not see the Sun. In reality; udayāstamanākhyaṃ hi darśanādarśanaṃ raveḥ — Sun does not rise or set as what we perceive here. It is by people seeing or not seeing that such things happen.

Śrīdhara

ato raverudayāstamanākhyaṃ darśanādarśanamātram eva — Śrīdhara gloss: ato raverudayāstamanākhyaṃ darśanādarśanamātram eva

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

naivāstamanamarkasya nodayaḥ sarvadā sataḥ | udayāstamanākhyaṃ hi darśanādarśanaṃ raveḥ ||15||

ato raverudayāstamanākhyaṃ darśanādarśanamātram eva

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अतो रवेरुदयास्तमनाख्यं दर्शनादर्शनमात्रम् एव

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: ato raverudayāstamanākhyaṃ darśanādarśanamātram eva

VP 2.8.16

शक्रादीनां पुरे तिष्ठन् स्पृशत्येष पुरत्रयम् । विकोणौ द्वौ विकोणस्थस्त्रीन् कोणान्द्वे पुरे तथा

He, being located on top of Indrapuri, he also touches three cities. When he is on top of the eastern direction, he touches the east, and then the north-eastern and south-eastern directions also.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

śakrādīnāṃ pure tiṣṭhan spṛśatyeṣa puratrayam — He, being located on top of Indrapuri, he also touches three cities. When he is on top of the eastern direction, he touches the east, and then the north-eastern and south-eastern directions also.; vikoṇau dvau vikoṇasthastrīn koṇāndve pure tathā — He, being located on top of Indrapuri, he also touches three cities. When he is on top of the eastern direction, he touches the east, and then the north-eastern and south-eastern directions also.

Śrīdhara

darśana-adarśanayoḥ avadhim āha — he states the limits of visibility and invisibility; śakrādīnām pure madhyāhne — when it is midday in one of the cities beginning with Śakra’s; ekaṃ puraṃ puradvayaṃ ca — one city and the two cities around it; vikarṇau koṇau — and the two diagonal corners; bhū-valayasya ardhe dṛśyate — half of the earth-circle is seen; ardhe tu na dṛśyate — the other half is not seen

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

śakrādīnāṃ pure tiṣṭhan spṛśatyeṣa puratrayam | vikoṇau dvau vikoṇasthastrīn koṇāndve pure tathā ||16||

darśanādarśanayor avadhim āha—śakrādīnām iti | indrādyanyatamasya pure madhyāhne tiṣṭhan tadekaṃ puram abhitaḥ puradvayañcety evaṃ puratrayaṃ tadantarālasthau dvau ca vikarṇau koṇau udayādyavasthābhiḥ spṛśati svaraśmibhirbhāsayati | tathā vikarṇasthaḥ agnyādyanyatamasya koṇe madhyāhne sthitastatkoṇamabhitaḥ sthitaṃ koṇadvayañcetvaṃ trīn koṇān tanmadhyavartinī dve ca pure tathaivodayādyavasthābhiḥ spṛśati | bhūvalayasyārddhe pratapan dṛśyate tāvadāha | ardhe tu na dṛśyate tāvatī rātrīrityarthaḥ | tathā hi aindre pure madhyāhne yadā tiṣṭhati, tadā saumyapurasthānāmastamayaḥ, īśānakoṇasthānāṃ tṛtīyo yāmaḥ | agnikoṇasthānāṃ prathamo yāmaḥ | yāmyasthitānāmudayaḥ | evaṃ yadā yāmye madhyāhne tiṣṭhati, tadā aindra'stamayaḥ, agnikoṇe tṛtīyo yāmaḥ, nirṛtikoṇe prathamo yāmaḥ, vāruṇe udayaḥ, yadā ca vāruṇe madhyāhnastadā yāmye astamayaḥ, nirṛtikoṇe tṛtīyo yāmaḥ, vāyavye prathamaḥ somye udayaḥ | yadā ca saumye madhyāhnastadā vāruṇe'stamayaḥ, vāyavye tṛtīyo yāmaḥ, īśakoṇe prathamo yāmaḥ, aindra udayaḥ | evamagnikoṇe yadā madhyāhnaḥ tadā īśānakoṇe astamayaḥ, indrapure tṛtīyo yāmaḥ | yamapure prathamaḥ, nirṛtikoṇe udaya ityādi yojyam | evaṃ meroḥ sarvataḥ paribhraman sūryo'rddhaṃ bhūvalayaṃ prakāśayan darśanā darśanādyapekṣāyāhorātravyavasthākāraṇamityuktaṃ bhavati

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

दर्शनादर्शनयोर् अवधिम् आह—शक्रादीनाम् इति । इन्द्राद्यन्यतमस्य पुरे मध्याह्ने तिष्ठन् तदेकं पुरम् अभितः पुरद्वयञ्चेत्य् एवं पुरत्रयं तदन्तरालस्थौ द्वौ च विकर्णौ कोणौ उदयाद्यवस्थाभिः स्पृशति स्वरश्मिभिर्भासयति । तथा विकर्णस्थः अग्न्याद्यन्यतमस्य कोणे मध्याह्ने स्थितस्तत्कोणमभितः स्थितं कोणद्वयञ्चेत्वं त्रीन् कोणान् तन्मध्यवर्तिनी द्वे च पुरे तथैवोदयाद्यवस्थाभिः स्पृशति । भूवलयस्यार्द्धे प्रतपन् दृश्यते तावदाह । अर्धे तु न दृश्यते तावती रात्रीरित्यर्थः । तथा हि ऐन्द्रे पुरे मध्याह्ने यदा तिष्ठति, तदा सौम्यपुरस्थानामस्तमयः, ईशानकोणस्थानां तृतीयो यामः । अग्निकोणस्थानां प्रथमो यामः । याम्यस्थितानामुदयः । एवं यदा याम्ये मध्याह्ने तिष्ठति, तदा ऐन्द्रऽस्तमयः, अग्निकोणे तृतीयो यामः, निरृतिकोणे प्रथमो यामः, वारुणे उदयः, यदा च वारुणे मध्याह्नस्तदा याम्ये अस्तमयः, निरृतिकोणे तृतीयो यामः, वायव्ये प्रथमः सोम्ये उदयः । यदा च सौम्ये मध्याह्नस्तदा वारुणेऽस्तमयः, वायव्ये तृतीयो यामः, ईशकोणे प्रथमो यामः, ऐन्द्र उदयः । एवमग्निकोणे यदा मध्याह्नः तदा ईशानकोणे अस्तमयः, इन्द्रपुरे तृतीयो यामः । यमपुरे प्रथमः, निरृतिकोणे उदय इत्यादि योज्यम् । एवं मेरोः सर्वतः परिभ्रमन् सूर्योऽर्द्धं भूवलयं प्रकाशयन् दर्शना दर्शनाद्यपेक्षायाहोरात्रव्यवस्थाकारणमित्युक्तं भवति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Atha etaam eva dik chakra ardha vyaaptim udaaharati shakraadeenaam iti | Purena dik upalakshyaa, vikonena vidik, shakra aadi anyatamasya pura upari sthitah tat puradisham tat abhitah sthitam dik dvayam, anantaram vidishou cha sprushati | In the front is the direction; east, west, etc. are dik, and in between are vidik. In front and on top of the city of Indra, he is seen in that location in that city, as well as around that the two more directions, he is visible. Then, he also touches the directions in between the two directions. Evam aagneyaadi vidik samsthah taam vidisham tat abhitah sthitam dik dvayam anantaram vidik dvayam cha bhaasayati, ashta dik mandale ardham bhaasayati iti arthah | Being located on Aagneya vidik (south-east), and around that whatever two directions are located, he is visible there also. He is visible in the teo directions, and also the two in between directions. In the eight directions, he is visible in half the directions at any point of time. Tat yathaa indrapuryaa oordhvam tishthan tatra madhyaahnam eesha konasthaanaam truteeyayaantam agni konasthaanaam agnyaamaantam yaamya purasthaanaam udayam soumya purasthaanaam astamayam karoti | On top of Indrapuri, it is madhyaahna. In the various directions, how it is rising, setting, how it is half way through, how it is one quarter of the day, it is the third quarter of the day, are told. Evam agnivikonastha tatrasthaanaam madhyaahnam yayaa indrapuryoh aadya truteeya yaama antou nirrutireesha konayoh udaya astamayou, cha karoti | Evam sarva dik vidikshu yojyam | In Nirruti and Eeshaanya, how it is rising and setting, is told. In the same way, we have to imagine for all the directions and vidiks. These are all various movements of the Sun, rising and setting. [[123]]

VP 2.8.17

उदितो वर्धमानाभिरामध्याह्नात्तपन्रविः । ततः परं ह्रसन्तीभिर्गोभिरस्तं नियच्छति

When he is rising, the Sun's rays are going on increasing till the midday, and then it reduces in intensity. And then, he sets in the evening. When the Sun is rising, till the afternoon, the brightness of Sun goes on increasing, his rays become more and more fierce and powerful. After that, it goes on reducing, and then he sets.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

udito vardhamānābhirāmadhyāhnāttapanraviḥ — When he is rising, the Sun's rays are going on increasing till the midday, and then it reduces in intensity. And then, he sets in the evening. When the Sun is rising, till the afternoon, the brightness of Sun goes on increasing, his rays become more and more fierce and powerful. After that, it goes on reducing, and then he sets.; tataḥ paraṃ hrasantībhirgobhirastaṃ niyacchati — When he is rising, the Sun's rays are going on increasing till the midday, and then it reduces in intensity. And then, he sets in the evening. When the Sun is rising, till the afternoon, the brightness of Sun goes on increasing, his rays become more and more fierce and powerful. After that, it goes on reducing, and then he sets.

Śrīdhara

sannidhānavyavadhānakṛtam eva raśmīnāṃ vṛddhihrāsatīvramandatvādikamapītyāha—udita iti | gobhiḥ raśmibhiḥ — Udita iti | Gaavo rashmayah |

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

udito vardhamānābhirāmadhyāhnāttapanraviḥ | tataḥ paraṃ hrasantībhirgobhirastaṃ niyacchati ||17||

sannidhānavyavadhānakṛtam eva raśmīnāṃ vṛddhihrāsatīvramandatvādikamapītyāha—udita iti | gobhiḥ raśmibhiḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

सन्निधानव्यवधानकृतम् एव रश्मीनां वृद्धिह्रासतीव्रमन्दत्वादिकमपीत्याह—उदित इति । गोभिः रश्मिभिः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Udita iti | Gaavo rashmayah |

VP 2.8.18

उदयास्तमनाभ्यां च स्मृते पूर्वापरे दिशौ । यावत्पुरस्तात्तपति तावत्पृष्ठे च पार्श्वयोः

Because of the rising and setting only, the eastern and western directions are also recognized. The east and west directions are said to be because of the rising of the Sun and its setting. The Sun is always shining equally in all directions, front, back, sides, everywhere. As he is shining like that, he covers the main two directions, and the two sub-directions, and the inter-directions.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

udayāstamanābhyāṃ ca smṛte pūrvāpare diśau — Because of the rising and setting only, the eastern and western directions are also recognized. The east and west directions are said to be because of the rising of the Sun and its setting. The Sun is always shining equally in all directions, front, back, sides, everywhere. As he is shining like that, he covers the main two directions, and the two sub-directions, and the inter-directions.; yāvatpurastāttapati tāvatpṛṣṭhe ca pārśvayoḥ — Because of the rising and setting only, the eastern and western directions are also recognized. The east and west directions are said to be because of the rising of the Sun and its setting. The Sun is always shining equally in all directions, front, back, sides, everywhere. As he is shining like that, he covers the main two directions, and the two sub-directions, and the inter-directions.

Śrīdhara

dig-vibhāgo udayāstamaya-nimittaḥ — the division of directions is determined by rising and setting; yatra yasya udeti sā tasya pūrvā dik — where it rises is its eastern direction; yatra astam eti sā pratīcī — where it sets is the western direction; dakṣiṇa-vāma-pārśva-bhāgau dakṣiṇa-uttare diśau — the right and left sides are the southern and northern directions; lokāloka-acala-paryantam tapatī — it shines as far as the Lokāloka mountain

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

udayāstamanābhyāṃ ca smṛte pūrvāpare diśau | yāvatpurastāttapati tāvatpṛṣṭhe ca pārśvayoḥ ||18||

digvibhāgo'pi udayāstamayanimitta euvetyāha—udaya iti | yatra yasyodeti, sā tasya pūrvā dik | yatrāstameti sā parā pratīcī | tathā ca śrutiḥ— tasmādasāvādityaḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ pratyagudeti | tasmāt sarva eva manyate māṃ pratyudagāt | iti | purastādudyantaṃ sūryaṃ paśyataśca dakṣiṇavāmapārśvabhāgau dakṣiṇottare diśāviti darśayan raśmivistārāvadhim āha—yāvad iti | caturdikṣu lokālokācalaparyantaṃ tapatītyarthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

दिग्विभागोऽपि उदयास्तमयनिमित्त एउवेत्याह—उदय इति । यत्र यस्योदेति, सा तस्य पूर्वा दिक् । यत्रास्तमेति सा परा प्रतीची । तथा च श्रुतिः— तस्मादसावादित्यः सर्वाः प्रजाः प्रत्यगुदेति । तस्मात् सर्व एव मन्यते मां प्रत्युदगात् । इति । पुरस्तादुद्यन्तं सूर्यं पश्यतश्च दक्षिणवामपार्श्वभागौ दक्षिणोत्तरे दिशाविति दर्शयन् रश्मिविस्तारावधिम् आह—यावद् इति । चतुर्दिक्षु लोकालोकाचलपर्यन्तं तपतीत्यर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: digvibhāgo'pi udayāstamayanimitta euvetyāha—udaya iti | yatra yasyodeti, sā tasya pūrvā dik | yatrāstameti sā parā pratīcī | tathā ca śrutiḥ— tasmādasāvādityaḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ pratyagudeti | tasmāt sarva eva manyate māṃ pratyudagāt | iti | purastādudyantaṃ sūryaṃ paśyataśca dakṣiṇavāmapārśvabhāgau dakṣiṇottare diśāviti darśayan raśmivistārāvadhim āha—yāvad iti | caturdikṣu lokālokācalaparyantaṃ tapatītyarthaḥ

VP 2.8.19

ऋतेऽमरगिरेर्मेरोरुपरि ब्रह्मणः सभाम् । येये मरीचयोर्कस्य प्रयान्ति ब्रह्मणः सभाम् । तेते निरस्तास्तद्भासा प्रतीपमुपयान्ति वै

Rays which are moving up towards the Brahma's city, they are all reflected back. Except the city of Brahman, sitting on top of the Meru parvata; whichever ray tries to reach the city of Brahma, they are returned back. The brightness of Brahman's place is so much that the rays of the Sun are nothing compared to that. There are additional explanations of the Sun's movement and the various hues we see in the evening and morning. Lot more things will come about Surya Mandala, which we will see next. These have a lot to do with Jyoutisha. We are studying Amsha 2, Chapter 8. We were studying how day and night happen, the movement of the Sun, the eight directions, four main directions and four inter directions. How, when the Sun moves, what are the areas covered by the Sun, were explained. [[124]]

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ṛte'maragirermerorupari brahmaṇaḥ sabhām — Rays which are moving up towards the Brahma's city, they are all reflected back. Except the city of Brahman, sitting on top of the Meru parvata; yeye marīcayorkasya prayānti brahmaṇaḥ sabhām — whichever ray tries to reach the city of Brahma, they are returned back. The brightness of Brahman's place is so much that the rays of the Sun are nothing compared to that. There are additional explanations of the Sun's movement and the various hues we see in the evening and morning. Lot more things will come about Surya Mandala, which we will see next. These have a lot to do with Jyoutisha. We are studying Amsha 2; tete nirastāstadbhāsā pratīpamupayānti vai — Chapter 8. We were studying how day and night happen, the movement of the Sun, the eight directions, four main directions and four inter directions. How, when the Sun moves, what are the areas covered by the Sun, were explained. [[124]]

Śrīdhara

merau tu viśeṣam āha — but he states a distinction regarding Meru; brahma-sabhā-varjam meru-upari api sarvataḥ tapati — except Brahmā's assembly, it shines everywhere above Meru; na tu brahma-sabhām bhāsayati — it does not illuminate Brahmā's assembly; tatra hetum āha — he gives the reason; ye ye marīcayaḥ — “whatever rays...”

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ṛte'maragirermerorupari brahmaṇaḥ sabhām | yeye marīcayorkasya prayānti brahmaṇaḥ sabhām | tete nirastāstadbhāsā pratīpamupayānti vai ||19||

mero tu viśeṣam āha—ṛta iti | brahmasabhāvarjaṃ meroruparyapi sarvatastapati na tu brahmasabhāṃ bhāsayati | tatra hetum āha—ye ye marīcaya iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

मेरो तु विशेषम् आह—ऋत इति । ब्रह्मसभावर्जं मेरोरुपर्यपि सर्वतस्तपति न तु ब्रह्मसभां भासयति । तत्र हेतुम् आह—ये ये मरीचय इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Udaya iti | Tat vruddhi hraasou aasatti viprakarsha kruta darshana adarshanaabhyaam | Udayena poorvaa dik, astamayena aparaa dik | Ata eva udyantam bhaasvantam pashyataam vaama dakshina bhaage sthitatvaat sarveshaam uttaro meruh lokaalokaachalah cha dakshinah syaat | Whenever the Sun is near, and when he is far away, the fierceness of Sun's rays are increasing or decreasing. When we see or don't see the Sun, the rising and setting are told. Wherever he is rising is the east direction. Wherever he is setting is identified as west. Because of that, whoever is seeing the Sun which is rising, for everyone, Meru is always on the north, and Lokaalokaachala is in the south.

VP 2.8.20

तस्माद्दिश्युत्तरस्यां वै दिवारात्रिः सदैव हि । सर्वेषां द्वीपवर्षाणां सेरुरुत्तरतो यतः

In the northern direction, there is always day and night. Meru is in the north of all the varshaas.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

tasmāddiśyuttarasyāṃ vai divārātriḥ sadaiva hi — In the northern direction, there is always day and night. Meru is in the north of all the varshaas.; sarveṣāṃ dvīpavarṣāṇāṃ seruruttarato yataḥ — In the northern direction, there is always day and night. Meru is in the north of all the varshaas.

Śrīdhara

meroḥ sarvato viśeṣāntaram — a further distinction concerning Meru on every side; sarveṣāṃ dvīpānāṃ varṣāṇāṃ ca meruḥ uttarataḥ — Meru is to the north of all islands and regions; uttarasyāṃ diśi sadā divā — in the northern direction there is always day; anyeṣāṃ dine api nityam rātriḥ — for the others there is always night even during their day; dakṣiṇasyāṃ sadā dinam — in the southern direction there is always day

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

tasmāddiśyuttarasyāṃ vai divārātriḥ sadaiva hi | sarveṣāṃ dvīpavarṣāṇāṃ seruruttarato yataḥ ||20||

meroḥ sarvato'pi viśeṣāntaram āha—tasmād iti | yato yasmāt sarveṣāṃ dvīpānāṃ varṣāṇāñca meruruttarata eva sthitaḥ | tasmānmeroruttarasyāṃ diśi sadā divāpi | anyeṣāṃ dine'pi nityaṃ rātrireva | ayaṃ bhāvaḥ-meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvantaṃ sūryaṃ ye yatra paśyanti, sā ca teṣāṃ prācī, teṣāñca vāmabhāga eva meruḥ | ataḥ sarveṣāṃ sarvadā meruruttara eva | dakṣiṇabhāge ca lokālokācalaḥ ॥ tasmāduttarasyāṃ diśi sadā rātriḥ | dakṣiṇasyāñca sadā dinam iti | yadvā bhāratādivarṣasthānāṃ sammukha sūryamudyantaṃ paśyatām uttarasyāṃ diśi vāmabhāge merorekataḥ sadā dinam, anyataśca sadā rātriḥ | dakṣiṇabhāge tu sadā dinamevetyetadarthāduktaṃ bhavati

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

मेरोः सर्वतोऽपि विशेषान्तरम् आह—तस्माद् इति । यतो यस्मात् सर्वेषां द्वीपानां वर्षाणाञ्च मेरुरुत्तरत एव स्थितः । तस्मान्मेरोरुत्तरस्यां दिशि सदा दिवापि । अन्येषां दिनेऽपि नित्यं रात्रिरेव । अयं भावः-मेरुं प्रदक्षिणीकुर्वन्तं सूर्यं ये यत्र पश्यन्ति, सा च तेषां प्राची, तेषाञ्च वामभाग एव मेरुः । अतः सर्वेषां सर्वदा मेरुरुत्तर एव । दक्षिणभागे च लोकालोकाचलः ॥ तस्मादुत्तरस्यां दिशि सदा रात्रिः । दक्षिणस्याञ्च सदा दिनम् इति । यद्वा भारतादिवर्षस्थानां सम्मुख सूर्यमुद्यन्तं पश्यताम् उत्तरस्यां दिशि वामभागे मेरोरेकतः सदा दिनम्, अन्यतश्च सदा रात्रिः । दक्षिणभागे तु सदा दिनमेवेत्येतदर्थादुक्तं भवति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Tasmaat iti | Tasmaat dishi uttarasyaam nitya uttarasya meroh upari dik bhaage brahma sabhaa prabhayaa sadaa divaatvam, tatra arka prabhaa sanchaara abhaavaat nitya raatritva upachaarah | Always, Meru parvata is in the northern direction. The brightness of the Brahman's place is always shining on that, and so there is always day. Because the brightness of Brahman's place is so much, the Sun's rays are negligible and not even seen, they don't reach that place at all; so it is said to be night as Sun's rays don't reach there at all. This is oupachaarika, figuratively told.

VP 2.8.21

प्रभा विवस्वतो रात्रावस्तं गच्छति भास्करे । विशत्यग्निमतो रात्रौ वह्निर्दूरात्प्रकाशते

In the night when the Sun sets, the brightness of the Sun enters into fire; because of this, Agni, fire is burning bright from a distance.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

prabhā vivasvato rātrāvastaṃ gacchati bhāskare — In the night when the Sun sets, the brightness of the Sun enters into fire; viśatyagnimato rātrau vahnirdūrātprakāśate — because of this

Śrīdhara

udayāstamayanirṇayaprasaṅgāt tatkālānugāgnihotre miśraliṅgamantraviśeṣabrāhmaṇoktamagnisūryayoḥ parasparā′nupraveśamupāsanārtham āha—prabheti tribhiḥ — Śrīdhara gloss: udayāstamayanirṇayaprasaṅgāt tatkālānugāgnihotre miśraliṅgamantraviśeṣabrāhmaṇoktamagnisūryayoḥ parasparā′nupraveśamupāsanārtham āha—prabheti tribhiḥ

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

prabhā vivasvato rātrāvastaṃ gacchati bhāskare | viśatyagnimato rātrau vahnirdūrātprakāśate ||21||

udayāstamayanirṇayaprasaṅgāt tatkālānugāgnihotre miśraliṅgamantraviśeṣabrāhmaṇoktamagnisūryayoḥ parasparā′nupraveśamupāsanārtham āha—prabheti tribhiḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

उदयास्तमयनिर्णयप्रसङ्गात् तत्कालानुगाग्निहोत्रे मिश्रलिङ्गमन्त्रविशेषब्राह्मणोक्तमग्निसूर्ययोः परस्परा′नुप्रवेशमुपासनार्थम् आह—प्रभेति त्रिभिः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: udayāstamayanirṇayaprasaṅgāt tatkālānugāgnihotre miśraliṅgamantraviśeṣabrāhmaṇoktamagnisūryayoḥ parasparā′nupraveśamupāsanārtham āha—prabheti tribhiḥ

VP 2.8.22

वह्नोः प्रभा तथा भानुर्दिनेष्वाविशति द्विज । अतीव वह्निसंयोगादतः सूर्यः प्रकाशते

In the same way, brightness of Agni enters into Sun during the daytime. Because Agni's part has entered into the Sun in the morning, Sun is also burning bright.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

vahnoḥ prabhā tathā bhānurdineṣvāviśati dvija — In the same way, brightness of Agni enters into Sun during the daytime. Because Agni's part has entered into the Sun in the morning; atīva vahnisaṃyogādataḥ sūryaḥ prakāśate — Sun is also burning bright.

Śrīdhara

vahniḥ pādaḥ caturthāṃśaḥ dineṣu bhānuṃ viśati — a quarter of the fire enters the Sun during the day; bhānoḥ uṣṇatve api adāhakatvam — therefore the Sun’s heat does not burn; vahneḥ ahni dāhakatvam — while fire burns during the day; agnir jyotiḥ jyotir agniḥ — fire is light and light is fire; sūryo jyotiḥ jyotiḥ sūryaḥ — the Sun is light and light is the Sun

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

vahnoḥ prabhā tathā bhānurdineṣvāviśati dvija | atīva vahnisaṃyogādataḥ sūryaḥ prakāśate ||22||

vahniḥ pādaścaturthāṃśo dineṣu bhānuṃ viśati | atastāvanmātrapraveśād bhānoruṣṇatve'pyadāhakatvaṃ, vahveścāhni dāhakatvamaviruddham | tathā ca agnirjyotirjyotiragniḥ svāhetyeva sāyaṃ hotavyaṃ, sūryo jyotirjyotiḥ sūryaḥ svāheti prātaḥ | ityedvidhiviśeṣe śrūyate | agni vāvādityaḥ sāyaṃ praviśati tasmādagnirdūrānnaktam | udyantaṃ vāvādityamagniranusamārohati tasmād dhūma evāgnerdivā dadṛśe | iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

वह्निः पादश्चतुर्थांशो दिनेषु भानुं विशति । अतस्तावन्मात्रप्रवेशाद् भानोरुष्णत्वेऽप्यदाहकत्वं, वह्वेश्चाह्नि दाहकत्वमविरुद्धम् । तथा च अग्निर्ज्योतिर्ज्योतिरग्निः स्वाहेत्येव सायं होतव्यं, सूर्यो ज्योतिर्ज्योतिः सूर्यः स्वाहेति प्रातः । इत्येद्विधिविशेषे श्रूयते । अग्नि वावादित्यः सायं प्रविशति तस्मादग्निर्दूरान्नक्तम् । उद्यन्तं वावादित्यमग्निरनुसमारोहति तस्माद् धूम एवाग्नेर्दिवा ददृशे । इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Atha udaya astamaya nirnaya prasakta sandhyaa anusthaana agnihotreeya samsrushta homa prathama aahuti shesham Yasya vai dvou punyou gruhe vasata ityaadi braahmanam anugruhnaati - prabhaa vivasvata ityaadina | Prabhaa iti | Soorya prabhaayaah chaturthaamsho nishi agnim vishati prabhaa hi souree paadena iti vaayu ukteh; vanhi paadah tathaa iti sva ukteh cha | Day and night are decided connected to that. At that time, Sandhyaavandanaa and other karmaas are done. Agnihotra is done in the morning. The same point is told in Taittiriya Braahmana. It is said that the brightness of Agni enters into the Sun, and the brightness of Sun enters into Agni. So, in Agnihotra, when they offer aahuti, they offer to both the gods Agni and Surya. Because when there are two respectable people, if we show respect to one and do not show respect to the other, it is as if the person to whom you have shown respect also is direspected, because the other person was not shown due respect. So, both Agni and Surya should be given aahuti. In morning and evening, for both aahuti is to be given. In Vaayu Puraana, it is told that one fourth part of brightness of the Sun enters into Agni in the night. In the same way, one fourth part of the Agni also enters into the Sun.

VP 2.8.23

तेजसी भास्कराग्नेये प्रकाशोष्णस्वरूपिणी । परस्परानुप्रवेशादाप्यायेते दिवानिशम्

Brightness of Sun and Agni, which are prakaasha and ushna, they mutually enter into each, and because of that, they brighten the day and night.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

tejasī bhāskarāgneye prakāśoṣṇasvarūpiṇī — Brightness of Sun and Agni, which are prakaasha and ushna, they mutually enter into each, and because of that, they brighten the day and night.; parasparānupraveśādāpyāyete divāniśam — Brightness of Sun and Agni, which are prakaasha and ushna, they mutually enter into each, and because of that, they brighten the day and night.

Śrīdhara

vahniḥ pādaḥ caturthāṃśaḥ dineṣu bhānuṃ viśati — a quarter of the fire enters the Sun during the day; bhānoḥ uṣṇatve api adāhakatvam — therefore the Sun’s heat does not burn; vahneḥ ahni dāhakatvam — while fire burns during the day; sāyaṃ agniḥ praviśati — in the evening the Sun enters fire; udyantam ādityam agniḥ anusamārohati — fire follows the rising Sun

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

tejasī bhāskarāgneye prakāśoṣṇasvarūpiṇī | parasparānupraveśādāpyāyete divāniśam ||23||

vahniḥ pādaścaturthāṃśo dineṣu bhānuṃ viśati | atastāvanmātrapraveśād bhānoruṣṇatve'pyadāhakatvaṃ, vahveścāhni dāhakatvamaviruddham | tathā ca agnirjyotirjyotiragniḥ svāhetyeva sāyaṃ hotavyaṃ, sūryo jyotirjyotiḥ sūryaḥ svāheti prātaḥ | ityedvidhiviśeṣe śrūyate | agni vāvādityaḥ sāyaṃ praviśati tasmādagnirdūrānnaktam | udyantaṃ vāvādityamagniranusamārohati tasmād dhūma evāgnerdivā dadṛśe | iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

वह्निः पादश्चतुर्थांशो दिनेषु भानुं विशति । अतस्तावन्मात्रप्रवेशाद् भानोरुष्णत्वेऽप्यदाहकत्वं, वह्वेश्चाह्नि दाहकत्वमविरुद्धम् । तथा च अग्निर्ज्योतिर्ज्योतिरग्निः स्वाहेत्येव सायं होतव्यं, सूर्यो ज्योतिर्ज्योतिः सूर्यः स्वाहेति प्रातः । इत्येद्विधिविशेषे श्रूयते । अग्नि वावादित्यः सायं प्रविशति तस्मादग्निर्दूरान्नक्तम् । उद्यन्तं वावादित्यमग्निरनुसमारोहति तस्माद् धूम एवाग्नेर्दिवा ददृशे । इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Atha udaya astamaya nirnaya prasakta sandhyaa anusthaana agnihotreeya samsrushta homa prathama aahuti shesham Yasya vai dvou punyou gruhe vasata ityaadi braahmanam anugruhnaati - prabhaa vivasvata ityaadina | Prabhaa iti | Soorya prabhaayaah chaturthaamsho nishi agnim vishati prabhaa hi souree paadena iti vaayu ukteh; vanhi paadah tathaa iti sva ukteh cha | Day and night are decided connected to that. At that time, Sandhyaavandanaa and other karmaas are done. Agnihotra is done in the morning. The same point is told in Taittiriya Braahmana. It is said that the brightness of Agni enters into the Sun, and the brightness of Sun enters into Agni. So, in Agnihotra, when they offer aahuti, they offer to both the gods Agni and Surya. Because when there are two respectable people, if we show respect to one and do not show respect to the other, it is as if the person to whom you have shown respect also is direspected, because the other person was not shown due respect. So, both Agni and Surya should be given aahuti. In morning and evening, for both aahuti is to be given. In Vaayu Puraana, it is told that one fourth part of brightness of the Sun enters into Agni in the night. In the same way, one fourth part of the Agni also enters into the Sun.

VP 2.8.24

दक्षिणोत्तरभूम्यर्ध समुत्तिष्ठति भास्करे । आहोरात्रं विशत्यम्भस्तमः प्राकाश्यशीलवत्

One half of the earth, in the southern and northern parts, day and night, both are told. When the Sun sets, the night enters into water, or the day enters into water. When it is night time, the day enters into waters. When it is day, the night enters into water.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

dakṣiṇottarabhūmyardha samuttiṣṭhati bhāskare — One half of the earth, in the southern and northern parts, day and night, both are told. When the Sun sets, the night enters into water, or the day enters into water. When it is night time, the day enters into waters. When it is day, the night enters into water.; āhorātraṃ viśatyambhastamaḥ prākāśyaśīlavat — One half of the earth, in the southern and northern parts, day and night, both are told. When the Sun sets, the night enters into water, or the day enters into water. When it is night time, the day enters into waters. When it is day, the night enters into water.

Śrīdhara

ahorātra-prasaṅgāt tayoḥ apsu praveśam — in the context of day and night, their entry into the waters; upāsanā-ādi-artham āha — he states this for worship and the like; meru-dakṣiṇa-bhūmi-ardhe bhāskare samuttiṣṭhati sati — when the Sun rises in the southern half of Meru's land; dine tamaḥ-śīlā rātriḥ ambhaḥ praviśati — in the day, the dark-natured night enters the waters; meru-uttara-bhūmi-ardhe bhāskare tiṣṭhati — when the Sun stands in the northern half of Meru's land; rātrau prākāśya-śīlam ahaḥ ambhaḥ viśati — at night, the bright-natured day enters the waters; prakāśyam prākāśakatvam — “brightness” means the state of illuminating

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

dakṣiṇottarabhūmyardha samuttiṣṭhati bhāskare | āhorātraṃ viśatyambhastamaḥ prākāśyaśīlavat ||24||

ahorātraprasaṅgāt tayor apsu praveśamupāsanādyartham āha—dakṣiṇa iti | merodakṣiṇabhūmyarddhe bhāskare samuttiṣṭhati sati dine tamaḥśīlā rātriḥ ambhaḥ praviśati, meroruttarabhūmyardhe ca bhāskare tiṣṭhati rātrau prākāśyaśīlam ahaḥ ambho viśatītyarthaḥ | prakāśyaṃ prākāśakatvam

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अहोरात्रप्रसङ्गात् तयोर् अप्सु प्रवेशमुपासनाद्यर्थम् आह—दक्षिण इति । मेरोदक्षिणभूम्यर्द्धे भास्करे समुत्तिष्ठति सति दिने तमःशीला रात्रिः अम्भः प्रविशति, मेरोरुत्तरभूम्यर्धे च भास्करे तिष्ठति रात्रौ प्राकाश्यशीलम् अहः अम्भो विशतीत्यर्थः । प्रकाश्यं प्राकाशकत्वम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Atha ahoraatra sandhi prasangena tadanukaaricchaayaatapasandhistha vasateevareesamjnyta japa grahana shesham Yat vai divaa bhavati ityaadi braahmanam anugruhnaati dakshina uttara iti saardha dvi shlokyaam | Dakshina uttara iti | Tamah prakaashya iti atra kramo na vivakshitah | Tena meroh dakshina bhoomi arddhre ravou tishthati tamah sheelaavatee raatrirambho vishati, tadaa tatratyaanaam ahah syaat | Udak bhoomi arddhaste tu arke prakaashakatva sheelaam ahah apo vishati, dakshinasthaanaam tadaa raatrih syaat | Meeting point of day and night is sandhyaa kaala. At that time, Vasateevari japa is to be done, this also comes in Taittiriya Samhitaa. The southern half of Meru parvata, when the Sun is there; night which is having darkness, enters into water. At that time, [[126]] for people located there, it becomes day. When the Sun is in the northern half of the earth, day which is bright enters into water; at that time, those located in the southern part for them it is night.

VP 2.8.25

आताम्रा हि भवत्यापो दिवानक्तप्रवेशनात् । दिनं विशति चैवांभो भास्करेस्तमुपेयुषि । तस्माच्छुक्ला भवन्त्यापो नक्तमह्नः प्रवेशनात्

When the darkness enters into water, day happens, water becomes like a copper colour. When the Sun is setting, day enters into water, so waters are becoming white, shining.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ātāmrā hi bhavatyāpo divānaktapraveśanāt — When the darkness enters into water, day happens, water becomes like a copper colour. When the Sun is setting, day enters into water, so waters are becoming white, shining.; dinaṃ viśati caivāṃbho bhāskarestamupeyuṣi — When the darkness enters into water, day happens, water becomes like a copper colour. When the Sun is setting, day enters into water, so waters are becoming white, shining.; tasmācchuklā bhavantyāpo naktamahnaḥ praveśanāt — When the darkness enters into water, day happens, water becomes like a copper colour. When the Sun is setting, day enters into water, so waters are becoming white, shining.

Śrīdhara

ātāmrāḥ āpaḥ divā — the waters are reddish by day; astaṃ upayūṣi — when the Sun has set; naktaṃ śuklāḥ āpaḥ — at night the waters are white; apo rātriṃ praviśati — the waters enter the night; apo ahaḥ praviśati — the waters enter the day

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ātāmrā hi bhavatyāpo divānaktapraveśanāt | dinaṃ viśati caivāṃbho bhāskarestamupeyuṣi | tasmācchuklā bhavantyāpo naktamahnaḥ praveśanāt ||25||

etadevopapādayati-ātāmrāhi iti sārddhena | ā īṣat tāmrāḥ divā dine | naktapraveśanād rātreḥ pravedādityarthaḥ | astamupeyuṣi astaṃ gate sati | naktaṃ rātrau | tathā ca śrutiḥ— yadvai divā bhavatyapo rātriṃ praviśati tasmāt tāmrā āpo divā dadṛśe | yannaktaṃ bhavatyapo'haḥ praviśati tasmāt śuklā āpo naktaṃ dadṛśe iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

एतदेवोपपादयति-आताम्राहि इति सार्द्धेन । आ ईषत् ताम्राः दिवा दिने । नक्तप्रवेशनाद् रात्रेः प्रवेदादित्यर्थः । अस्तमुपेयुषि अस्तं गते सति । नक्तं रात्रौ । तथा च श्रुतिः— यद्वै दिवा भवत्यपो रात्रिं प्रविशति तस्मात् ताम्रा आपो दिवा ददृशे । यन्नक्तं भवत्यपोऽहः प्रविशति तस्मात् शुक्ला आपो नक्तं ददृशे इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Aataamraa hi iti | Divaa dine, nakram raatrou |

VP 2.8.26

एवं पुष्करमध्येन यदा याति दिवाकरः । त्रिंशद्भागस्तु मेदिन्यास्तदा मौहूर्तिकी गतिः

From the centre of the Pushkara dveepa, when the Sun is moving, 1/30 part of the earth, when the Sun covers, this is said to be one muhoorta.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

evaṃ puṣkaramadhyena yadā yāti divākaraḥ — From the centre of the Pushkara dveepa, when the Sun is moving, 1/30 part of the earth, when the Sun covers, this is said to be one muhoorta.; triṃśadbhāgastu medinyāstadā mauhūrtikī gatiḥ — From the centre of the Pushkara dveepa, when the Sun is moving, 1/30 part of the earth, when the Sun covers, this is said to be one muhoorta.

Śrīdhara

meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvataḥ raveḥ muhūrta-gamyaṃ mārgaṃ — the path covered by the Sun in one muhūrta while circumambulating Meru; puṣkara-dvīpa-madhye — in the middle of Puṣkara Island; mānasottara-parimaṇḍala-lakṣitāyāḥ medinyāḥ — of the earth marked by the circumference of Mānasottara; mauhūrtikī gatiḥ — the hourly movement; ahorātrāt pataṅgasya gatiḥ — the Sun’s movement in a day and night

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

evaṃ puṣkaramadhyena yadā yāti divākaraḥ | triṃśadbhāgastu medinyāstadā mauhūrtikī gatiḥ ||26||

evaṃ meruṃ pradakṣiṇīkurvato ravermuhūrtagamyaṃ mārgaṃ kathayan uttarāyaṇaviṣuvaddakṣiṇāyanagatibhirahorātrāṇāṃ dairdhyasāmyalaghutvāni nirūpayati–evam ityādinā | uṣā rātrirityataḥ prāktanena granthena | puṣkaradvīpamadhye rathacakrādhārabhūta mānasottaraparimaṇḍalopalakṣitāyā medinyāstriṃśattamaṃ bhāgaṃ raviryadā yāti, tadā muhūrtasambandhinī gatiḥ | tadāha vāyuḥ— nava koṭya samākhyātā yojanaiḥ parimaṇḍalam | tathā śatasahasrāṇi catvāriṃśacca pañca ca | ahorātrāt pataṅgasya gatireṣā vidhīyate ॥ catvāriṃśacca pañca ceti cakārāt ṣaḍlakṣaṇāni jñeyāni | anyathā parimaṇḍalāpūrteḥ | kiñca— pūrṇāḥ śatasahasrāṇāmekatriṃśat tu tāḥ smṛtāḥ | pañcāśat tu tathānyāni sahasrāṇyadhikāni ca | mauhūrtikī gatirhyeṣā sūryasya tvabhidhīyate | iti | adhikāni ceti viṃśatisahasrāṇīti jñeyam

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

एवं मेरुं प्रदक्षिणीकुर्वतो रवेर्मुहूर्तगम्यं मार्गं कथयन् उत्तरायणविषुवद्दक्षिणायनगतिभिरहोरात्राणां दैर्ध्यसाम्यलघुत्वानि निरूपयति–एवम् इत्यादिना । उषा रात्रिरित्यतः प्राक्तनेन ग्रन्थेन । पुष्करद्वीपमध्ये रथचक्राधारभूत मानसोत्तरपरिमण्डलोपलक्षिताया मेदिन्यास्त्रिंशत्तमं भागं रविर्यदा याति, तदा मुहूर्तसम्बन्धिनी गतिः । तदाह वायुः— नव कोट्य समाख्याता योजनैः परिमण्डलम् । तथा शतसहस्राणि चत्वारिंशच्च पञ्च च । अहोरात्रात् पतङ्गस्य गतिरेषा विधीयते ॥ चत्वारिंशच्च पञ्च चेति चकारात् षड्लक्षणानि ज्ञेयानि । अन्यथा परिमण्डलापूर्तेः । किञ्च— पूर्णाः शतसहस्राणामेकत्रिंशत् तु ताः स्मृताः । पञ्चाशत् तु तथान्यानि सहस्राण्यधिकानि च । मौहूर्तिकी गतिर्ह्येषा सूर्यस्य त्वभिधीयते । इति । अधिकानि चेति विंशतिसहस्राणीति ज्ञेयम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Evam udak kaashtaayaam shata dveepa uttaraantaparidhi trimshaamshah vishuve ksheeroda uttaraan taparidhi trimshaamsho dvou mouhoortikee gatih vaayu proktaadi prasiddhih jnyeyaa | When Sun is causing day and night, at the centre of the pushkara, on the Manasottara parvata, the movement of chariot of Sun in a circular way, the central circumference of the Manasottara parvata, one-thirtieth part is said to be one muhoorta; this is told in Vaayu Puraana. The measure of the circumference is said to be 9 crore, 45 lakhs yojanaas. Sun covers this distance every day. One muhoorta is said to be 31 lakh, and 50,000 yojanaas. When this is multiplied by 30, we get 9 crore, 45 lakhs yojanaas, which is the complete circumference. In the centre of the Pushkara, he is going around during Dakshinaayana. From the northern end, he comes to the northern edge of the Shatadveepa in one muhoorta, and comes to the Ksheeroda parvata in another muhoorta. [[127]]

VP 2.8.27

कुलालचक्रपर्यन्ते भ्रमन्नेष दिवाकरः । करोत्यहस्तथा रात्रिं विमुञ्चन्मेदिनीं द्विजः

A potter has got a wheel in which he makes the pot, and it is rotating. A small insect which is sitting on the edge of a wheel; in the same way, the Sun is rotating, in a circular manner. In the same way, he makes day and night for the earth.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kulālacakraparyante bhramanneṣa divākaraḥ — A potter has got a wheel in which he makes the pot, and it is rotating. A small insect which is sitting on the edge of a wheel; karotyahastathā rātriṃ vimuñcanmedinīṃ dvijaḥ — in the same way, the Sun is rotating, in a circular manner. In the same way, he makes day and night for the earth.

Śrīdhara

jantoḥ kulāla-cakra-sambaddhatvāt — because the creature is connected with the potter's wheel; kulāla-cakra-paryante sthitaḥ jantuḥ yathā — as a creature standing at the circumference of a potter's wheel; tadvat dakṣiṇāyana-avasāne — likewise at the end of the southern course; jyotiṣ-cakra-avasāne bhraman tu — revolving at the edge of the stellar wheel; ekaika-muhūrtena medinyāḥ triṃśad-bhāgam vimuñcan — releasing one-thirtieth of the earth in each muhūrta; krameṇa uttara-āyaṇam ārabhya ahan ca rātriṃ ca — gradually beginning the northern course, day and night

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kulālacakraparyante bhramanneṣa divākaraḥ | karotyahastathā rātriṃ vimuñcanmedinīṃ dvijaḥ ||27||

jantoḥ kulālacakrasambaddhatvāt kulālacakraparyante sthito janturyathetyarthaḥ | tadvat dakṣiṇāyanāvasāne jyotiścakrāvasāne bhramaṃstvekaikamuhūrtena medinyāstriṃśadbhāgaṃ vimuñcan krama uttarāyaṇamārabhyāhañca rātriñca pūrvavaiparītyena karoti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

जन्तोः कुलालचक्रसम्बद्धत्वात् कुलालचक्रपर्यन्ते स्थितो जन्तुर्यथेत्यर्थः । तद्वत् दक्षिणायनावसाने ज्योतिश्चक्रावसाने भ्रमंस्त्वेकैकमुहूर्तेन मेदिन्यास्त्रिंशद्भागं विमुञ्चन् क्रम उत्तरायणमारभ्याहञ्च रात्रिञ्च पूर्ववैपरीत्येन करोति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Kulaala chakra paryantam iti | Kulaala chakra praantastha jantuh iva jyotischakra paryantastho arko medineem praak uktaam maanaasaadri paridhikaam dakshina kaashtha aatmikaam vimunchan dakshina kaashthaantya dina apekshayaa vishamam ahoraatram cha kurvan uttarottaram maargam cha gacchan makaraadi raashi shatkam mahaakshastham tathaa praak gatyaa bhachakraardham cha yaati | Like an insect which is sitting at the edge of a wheel of a potter, he is located at the outer edge of the nakshatra mandala, the 9 crore, 45 lakhs yojanaas of circumference which was told, to the Maanasaadri; in the southern edge, on the last day of the Dakshinaayana, day and night are unequal, and he moves towards the northern direction; he enters Makara and six raashis. He moves in the eastern direction covering the six raashis starting with Makara, and half of the nakshatra mandala. This is located in the great axle, mahaa aksha.

VP 2.8.28

अयनस्योत्तरस्यादौ मकरं याति भास्करः । ततः कुम्भं च मीनं च राशे राश्यन्तरं द्विज

In the northern half, he reaches Makara raashi.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ayanasyottarasyādau makaraṃ yāti bhāskaraḥ — In the northern half, he reaches Makara raashi.; tataḥ kumbhaṃ ca mīnaṃ ca rāśe rāśyantaraṃ dvija — In the northern half, he reaches Makara raashi.

Śrīdhara

tad eva pratyaham ahar-vṛddhyā rātri-hrāsena ca darśayann āha — showing the same process through the daily increase of day and decrease of night, he says; ayanasyottarasyādau makaraṃ yāti bhāskaraḥ — at the beginning of the northern course, the Sun reaches Capricorn; tataḥ kumbhaṃ ca mīnaṃ ca — then Aquarius and Pisces; rāśeḥ rāśy-antaram — one zodiac sign after another

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ayanasyottarasyādau makaraṃ yāti bhāskaraḥ | tataḥ kumbhaṃ ca mīnaṃ ca rāśe rāśyantaraṃ dvija ||28||

tad eva pratyahamaharvṛddhyā rātrihrāsena ca darśayannāha—ayanasya iti sārddhadvābhyām

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तद् एव प्रत्यहमहर्वृद्ध्या रात्रिह्रासेन च दर्शयन्नाह—अयनस्य इति सार्द्धद्वाभ्याम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains the Sun’s northward course through the zodiac. At its beginning the Sun enters Capricorn, then Aquarius and Pisces, while the length of day increases and the length of night decreases.

VP 2.8.29

त्रिष्वेतेष्वथ भुक्तेषु ततो वैषुवतीं गतिम् । प्रयाति सविता कुर्वन्नहोरात्रं ततः समम्

Trishu eteshu atha bhukteshu tato vaishuvateem gatim | Prayaati savitaa kurvan ahoraatram tatah samam | Vishuva is the middle part of Dakshinaayana or Uttaraayana. He makes the day and night equal.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

triṣveteṣvatha bhukteṣu tato vaiṣuvatīṃ gatim — Trishu eteshu atha bhukteshu tato vaishuvateem gatim | Prayaati savitaa kurvan ahoraatram tatah samam | Vishuva is the middle part of Dakshinaayana or Uttaraayana. He makes the day and night equal.; prayāti savitā kurvannahorātraṃ tataḥ samam — Trishu eteshu atha bhukteshu tato vaishuvateem gatim | Prayaati savitaa kurvan ahoraatram tatah samam | Vishuva is the middle part of Dakshinaayana or Uttaraayana. VP 2.8.30

ततो रात्रिः क्षयं याति वर्धतेऽनुदिनं दिनम्

Just as the outer circumference of the potter's wheel is moving very fast, when Sun is approaching the Dakshinaayana he is moving very fast. [[130]]

Word-for-word

VP 2.8.33

अतिवेगितया कालं वायुवेगबलाच्चरन् । तस्मात्प्रकष्टां भूमिं तु कालेनाल्पेन गच्छति

VP 2.8.34

सूर्यो द्वादशभिः शैघ्र्यान्मुहूर्ते दक्षिणायने । त्रयोदशार्धमृक्षाणामह्ना तु चरति द्विज । मुहूर्तैस्तावदृक्षाणि नक्तमष्टादशैश्चरन्

Sun moves very fast in Dakshinaayana, he covers thirteen and a half nakshatraas in 12 muhoortaas. And in the night, he needs 18 muhoortaas to cover. So, nights are longer.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

sūryo dvādaśabhiḥ śaighryānmuhūrte dakṣiṇāyane — Sun moves very fast in Dakshinaayana, he covers thirteen and a half nakshatraas in 12 muhoortaas. And in the night, he needs 18 muhoortaas to cover. So, nights are longer.; trayodaśārdhamṛkṣāṇāmahnā tu carati dvija — Sun moves very fast in Dakshinaayana, he covers thirteen and a half nakshatraas in 12 muhoortaas. And in the night, he needs 18 muhoortaas to cover. So, nights are longer.; muhūrtaistāvadṛkṣāṇi naktamaṣṭādaśaiścaran — Sun moves very fast in Dakshinaayana, he covers thirteen and a half nakshatraas in 12 muhoortaas. And in the night, he needs 18 muhoortaas to cover. So, nights are longer.

Śrīdhara

ṛkṣāṇāṃ trayodaśārddhaṃ — thirteen and a half stars; sārdhatrayodaśanakṣatropalakṣitaṃ — marked by thirteen and a half constellations; jyotiścakrārddham ityarthaḥ — this means half the celestial circle; tāvadṛkṣāṇi — that many stars; sārddhatrayādaśanakṣatrāṇi — thirteen and a half constellations; naktaṃ rātrau — at night; aṣṭādaśabhiścaran — moving through eighteen; idañca dvādaśāṣṭādaśamuhūrttatvam — and this twelve-and-eighteen-muhūrta measure; ahorātrayor — of day and night; deśaviśeṣāpekṣayoktaṃ — is stated depending on particular locations; na sarvatreti jñeyam — should be understood as not applying everywhere

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

sūryo dvādaśabhiḥ śaighryānmuhūrte dakṣiṇāyane | trayodaśārdhamṛkṣāṇāmahnā tu carati dvija | muhūrtaistāvadṛkṣāṇi naktamaṣṭādaśaiścaran ||34||

ṛkṣāṇāṃ trayodaśārddhaṃ sārdhatrayodaśanakṣatropalakṣitaṃ jyotiścakrārddhamityarthaḥ | tāvadṛkṣāṇi sārddhatrayādaśanakṣatrāṇi naktaṃ rātrāvaṣṭādaśabhiścaran idañca dvādaśāṣṭādaśamuhūrttatvamahorātrayor deśaviśeṣāpekṣayoktaṃ, na sarvatreti jñeyam

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ऋक्षाणां त्रयोदशार्द्धं सार्धत्रयोदशनक्षत्रोपलक्षितं ज्योतिश्चक्रार्द्धमित्यर्थः । तावदृक्षाणि सार्द्धत्रयादशनक्षत्राणि नक्तं रात्रावष्टादशभिश्चरन् इदञ्च द्वादशाष्टादशमुहूर्त्तत्वमहोरात्रयोर् देशविशेषापेक्षयोक्तं, न सर्वत्रेति ज्ञेयम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Etat eva udaaharan prapanchayati - sooryo dvaadashabhih ityaadinaa |

VP 2.8.35

कुलालचक्रमध्यस्थो यथा मन्दं प्रसर्पति । तथोदगयने सूर्यः सर्पते मन्दविक्रमः

In between the outer circumference and inner nave, that is half way through the radius, if the Sun is located in such a position, he goes slower than when he is located in the outer circumference. His movement becomes slower and slower as he moves towards the centre. In the same way, in Uttaraayana, he moves slowly.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kulālacakramadhyastho yathā mandaṃ prasarpati — In between the outer circumference and inner nave, that is half way through the radius, if the Sun is located in such a position, he goes slower than when he is located in the outer circumference. His movement becomes slower and slower as he moves towards the centre. In the same way, in Uttaraayana, he moves slowly.; tathodagayane sūryaḥ sarpate mandavikramaḥ — In between the outer circumference and inner nave, that is half way through the radius, if the Sun is located in such a position, he goes slower than when he is located in the outer circumference. His movement becomes slower and slower as he moves towards the centre. In the same way, in Uttaraayana, he moves slowly.

Śrīdhara

uttarāyaṇe tu ahorātrayoḥ pūrva-vaiparītyam — during the northward course, day and night are opposite to their former proportion; kulāla-cakra-madhya-sthaḥ — situated in the middle of a potter’s wheel; yathā mandaṃ prasarpati — as it moves slowly; madhya-sthaḥ nābhi-nemi-antarāla-sthaḥ — being situated between the hub and the rim

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kulālacakramadhyastho yathā mandaṃ prasarpati | tathodagayane sūryaḥ sarpate mandavikramaḥ ||35||

uttarāyaṇe tvahorātrayoḥ pūrvavaiparītyaṃ darśayati kulālacakramadhyastha iti sārdhaistribhiḥ | madhyasthaḥ nābhinemyantarālasthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

उत्तरायणे त्वहोरात्रयोः पूर्ववैपरीत्यं दर्शयति कुलालचक्रमध्यस्थ इति सार्धैस्त्रिभिः । मध्यस्थः नाभिनेम्यन्तरालस्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary compares the Sun’s motion to a point moving through the middle space of a potter’s wheel. During the northward course, the Sun’s slower movement produces longer days and shorter nights.

VP 2.8.37

अहर्भवति तच्चापि चरते मन्दविक्रमः

To cover 13.5 nakshatraas, he needs 18 muhoortaas during the day, as he is said to be moving slower; and only 12 muhoortaas during the night, in the Uttaraayana.
