VP 2.8.39

अतो मन्दतरं नाभ्यां चक्रं भ्रमति वै यथा । मृत्पिण्ड इव मध्यस्थो ध्रुवो भ्रमति वै तथा

[[132]] In the naabhi, nave, the movement is still slower.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ato mandataraṃ nābhyāṃ cakraṃ bhramati vai yathā — [[132]] In the naabhi, nave, the movement is still slower.; mṛtpiṇḍa iva madhyastho dhruvo bhramati vai tathā — [[132]] In the naabhi, nave, the movement is still slower.

Śrīdhara

dhruvasya tu tathā gati-vaiṣamyaṃ na asti — Dhruva has no such inequality of motion; kulāla-cakraṃ nābhyāṃ mandataraṃ yathā bhramati — as a potter's wheel moves more slowly at its hub; tatra-sthaḥ mṛt-piṇḍaḥ api — the lump of clay situated there; yathā mandataram eva bhramati — likewise moves even more slowly; tathā jyotiṣ-cakra-nābhiḥ — so too the hub of the wheel of luminaries; tatra-sthaḥ dhruvaḥ ca — and Dhruva situated there

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ato mandataraṃ nābhyāṃ cakraṃ bhramati vai yathā | mṛtpiṇḍa iva madhyastho dhruvo bhramati vai tathā ||39||

dhruvasya tu tathā gativaiṣamyaṃ nāstītyāha—atho iti dvābhyām | kulālacakraṃ nābhyāṃ mandataraṃ yathā bhramati atastatrastho mṛtpiṇḍo'pi yathā mandataram eva bhramati, tathā jyotiścakranābhistatrastho dhruvaśca

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ध्रुवस्य तु तथा गतिवैषम्यं नास्तीत्याह—अथो इति द्वाभ्याम् । कुलालचक्रं नाभ्यां मन्दतरं यथा भ्रमति अतस्तत्रस्थो मृत्पिण्डोऽपि यथा मन्दतरम् एव भ्रमति, तथा ज्योतिश्चक्रनाभिस्तत्रस्थो ध्रुवश्च

Śrīdhara commentary.

Ata iti | Kulaala chakra madhyastho mrut pinda iva jyotischakra madhyastho dhruvah kulaa chakram yathaa naabhou mandam bhramati tathaa bhramati | Just as a potters wheel is moving very slowly at the nave, in the same way, Dhruva is also rotating like that, as he is located at the centre of the nave.

VP 2.8.40

कुलालचक्रनाभिस्तु यथा तत्रैववर्तते । ध्रुवस्तथा हि मैत्रेय तत्रैव परिवर्तते

Dhruva is located at the nave. Wherever he is there, he is rotating there itself.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kulālacakranābhistu yathā tatraivavartate — Dhruva is located at the nave. Wherever he is there, he is rotating there itself.; dhruvastathā hi maitreya tatraiva parivartate — Dhruva is located at the nave. Wherever he is there, he is rotating there itself.

Śrīdhara

yathā ca nābhiḥ tatraiva parivartate — just as the hub of the wheel revolves in that very place; nābhistho mṛtpiṇḍaḥ tat-sthānaṃ na tyajati — a lump of clay placed at the hub does not leave its position; tathaiva dhruvaḥ paribhramann api — so Dhruva, although revolving; tat-sthānaṃ na tyajati — does not leave his position

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kulālacakranābhistu yathā tatraivavartate | dhruvastathā hi maitreya tatraiva parivartate ||40||

yathā ca nābhistatraiva parivṛtternābhistho mṛtpiṇḍastatsthānaṃ na tyajati, tathaiva dhruvaḥ paribhramannapi tatsthānaṃ na tyajatītyarthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

यथा च नाभिस्तत्रैव परिवृत्तेर्नाभिस्थो मृत्पिण्डस्तत्स्थानं न त्यजति, तथैव ध्रुवः परिभ्रमन्नपि तत्स्थानं न त्यजतीत्यर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains that Dhruva remains fixed in his place even while the celestial wheel revolves around him, just as a lump of clay at a potter’s hub does not leave the hub.

VP 2.8.41

उभयोः काष्ठयोर्मध्ये भ्रमतो मण्डलानि तु । दिवा नक्तं च सूर्यस्य मन्दा शीघ्राच वै गतिः

movement (the vernal equinox), when he makes the day and night of equal duration. Thenceforward the length of the night decreases, and the

evaṃ tāvannakṣatramuhūrtabhedairahorātravaicitryamuktam | idānīṃ tad eva rāśibhāgavaśāt prapañcayatrāha—ubhayor iti ṣaḍbhiḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: evaṃ tāvannakṣatramuhūrtabhedairahorātravaicitryamuktam | idānīṃ tad eva rāśibhāgavaśāt prapañcayatrāha—ubhayor iti ṣaḍbhiḥ

VP 2.8.47

दक्षिणे त्वयने चैव विपरीतां विवस्वतः

Śrīdhara

rāśi-pramāṇa-bhedasya ahorātra-vaicitrye hetutvam — differences in the measure of the signs cause variation in day and night; ekaiko rāśiḥ sapāda-nakṣatra-dvaya-ātmakaḥ — each sign consists of two and a quarter constellations; mīna-meṣau hrasvau — Pisces and Aries are short; makara-mithune dīrghe — Capricorn and Gemini are long; karkaṭa-cāpe dīrghatame — Cancer and Sagittarius are the longest; kanyā-tule same — Virgo and Libra are equal; dina-rātri-vṛddhi-hrāsau rāśi-pramāṇa-ādhīnau — the increase and decrease of day and night depend on the measure of the signs

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: rāśipramāṇabhedasyāhorātravaicitrye hetutvam āha—dināneder iti | tadbhogenaiva jyotiścakradvādaśo'ṃśaḥ sapādanakṣatradvayātmaka ekaiko rāśiḥ | tatra ca prāyaśo mīna-meṣau hrasvau | vṛkṣa-kumbhau tataḥ kiñcidadhikau | makara-mithune tataḥ kiñciddīrghe | siṃha-vṛścikau dīrghatarau | karkaṭa-cāpe dīrghatame | same kanyā-tule | tatrārkākrāntarāśiṃ samārabhya ṣaḍāśikṣetradarśanakālo dinam | itararāśiṣaṭkadarśanakālo rātriḥ | tadātra rāśipramāṇādhīnau dinarātrivṛddhihrāsāvityādi-jyotiḥśāstraprakriyāpi purāṇāviruddhā cānusandheyeti | mandāhni yasminnanayan ityane noktaṃ gativaicitryaṃ viśeṣato darśayati-uttara iti

VP 2.8.49

संध्याकाले च संप्राप्ते रौद्रे परमदारुणे । मन्देहा राक्षसा घोराः सूर्यमिच्छिन्ति खादितुम्

It is told in the Shruti that when the Sandhyaa kaala approaches, it is a very fierce time, very dangerous; there are raakshasaas by name Mandehaas, who are coming to eat the Sun. This is told in the Taittiriya Aaranyaka in the second Prashna.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

saṃdhyākāle ca saṃprāpte raudre paramadāruṇe — It is told in the Shruti that when the Sandhyaa kaala approaches, it is a very fierce time, very dangerous; mandehā rākṣasā ghorāḥ sūryamicchinti khāditum — there are raakshasaas by name Mandehaas, who are coming to eat the Sun. This is told in the Taittiriya Aaranyaka in the second Prashna.

Śrīdhara

tatra sandhyopāstimakurvataḥ sūryahatyādoṣaḥ syād iti nindārthavādapūrvakaṃ sandhyopāstiprakāram āha—sandhyākāle tv iti caturbhiḥ — Rakshaamsi ha vaa puraa anuvaaka, ityaadi svaadhyaaya braahmanam anugruhnaati sandhyaa kaale tu iti | The second prashna of Taittiriya Aaranyaka is called Svaadhyaaya Braahmana. When the arghya is given in Sandhyaa kaala, it throws the Mandeha raakshasaas away. It is told that one has to do Sandhyaavandana during that time, one has to give arghya, with the Gaayatri Mantra.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

saṃdhyākāle ca saṃprāpte raudre paramadāruṇe | mandehā rākṣasā ghorāḥ sūryamicchinti khāditum ||49||

tatra sandhyopāstimakurvataḥ sūryahatyādoṣaḥ syād iti nindārthavādapūrvakaṃ sandhyopāstiprakāram āha—sandhyākāle tv iti caturbhiḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत्र सन्ध्योपास्तिमकुर्वतः सूर्यहत्यादोषः स्याद् इति निन्दार्थवादपूर्वकं सन्ध्योपास्तिप्रकारम् आह—सन्ध्याकाले त्व् इति चतुर्भिः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Rakshaamsi ha vaa puraa anuvaaka, ityaadi svaadhyaaya braahmanam anugruhnaati sandhyaa kaale tu iti | The second prashna of Taittiriya Aaranyaka is called Svaadhyaaya Braahmana. When the arghya is given in Sandhyaa kaala, it throws the Mandeha raakshasaas away. It is told that one has to do Sandhyaavandana during that time, one has to give arghya, with the Gaayatri Mantra.

VP 2.8.50

प्रजापतिकृतः शापस्तेषां मैत्रेय रक्षसाम् । अक्षयत्वं शरीराणां मरणं च दिने दिने

Because of that, everyday they go and fight with Surya, and try to swallow him. At that time, all the braahmanaas, shreshthaas give the arghya, chanting the Gaayatri mantra.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

prajāpatikṛtaḥ śāpasteṣāṃ maitreya rakṣasām — Because of that, everyday they go and fight with Surya, and try to swallow him. At that time, all the braahmanaas, shreshthaas give the arghya, chanting the Gaayatri mantra.; akṣayatvaṃ śarīrāṇāṃ maraṇaṃ ca dine dine — Because of that, everyday they go and fight with Surya, and try to swallow him. At that time, all the braahmanaas, shreshthaas give the arghya, chanting the Gaayatri mantra.

Śrīdhara

prajāpati-dattaḥ varaḥ api duḥkha-phalatvāt śāpaḥ ucyate — a boon granted by Prajāpati is called a curse because it bears painful fruit; tathā ca śrutiḥ — so too the śruti; rakṣāṃsi puronuvāke tapaḥ ugram atiṣṭhanta — the Rākṣasas performed fierce austerity in the Puronuvāka; tān prajāpatiḥ vareṇa upāmantrayata — Prajāpati addressed them with a boon; tāni varam vṛṇīta — they chose a boon; ādityaḥ naḥ yoddhā hanti — “the Sun, our opponent, kills us”; tān prajāpatiḥ abravīt yodhayadhvam — Prajāpati said to them, “Fight”

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

prajāpatikṛtaḥ śāpasteṣāṃ maitreya rakṣasām | akṣayatvaṃ śarīrāṇāṃ maraṇaṃ ca dine dine ||50||

prajāpatidatto varo'pi duḥkhaphalatvāt śāpa ucyate | tathā ca śrutiḥ-rakṣāṃsi ha vā puronuvāke tapogramatiṣṭhanta, tān prajāpatirvareṇopāmantrayata tāni varaṃ vṛṇītādityo no yoddhā hati tān prajāpatirabravīd yodhayadhvame ityādi

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

प्रजापतिदत्तो वरोऽपि दुःखफलत्वात् शाप उच्यते । तथा च श्रुतिः-रक्षांसि ह वा पुरोनुवाके तपोग्रमतिष्ठन्त, तान् प्रजापतिर्वरेणोपामन्त्रयत तानि वरं वृणीतादित्यो नो योद्धा हति तान् प्रजापतिरब्रवीद् योधयध्वमे इत्यादि

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: prajāpatidatto varo'pi duḥkhaphalatvāt śāpa ucyate | tathā ca śrutiḥ-rakṣāṃsi ha vā puronuvāke tapogramatiṣṭhanta, tān prajāpatirvareṇopāmantrayata tāni varaṃ vṛṇītādityo no yoddhā hati tān prajāpatirabravīd yodhayadhvame ityādi

VP 2.8.51

ततः सूर्यस्य तैर्युद्धं भवत्यत्यन्तदारुणम् । ततो द्विजोत्तमा स्तोयं संक्षिपन्ति महामुने

the centre of the potter's wheel moves most slowly, so the polar-star, which is in the centre of the zodiacal

tataḥ sūryasya tairyuddhaṃ bhavatyatyantadāruṇam — the centre of the potter's wheel moves most slowly, so the polar-star, which is in the centre of the zodiacal; tato dvijottamā stoyaṃ saṃkṣipanti mahāmune — the centre of the potter's wheel moves most slowly, so the polar-star, which is in the centre of the zodiacal

Śrīdhara

prajāpati-dattaḥ varaḥ api duḥkha-phalatvāt śāpaḥ ucyate — a boon granted by Prajāpati is called a curse because it bears painful fruit; tathā ca śrutiḥ — so too the śruti; rakṣāṃsi puronuvāke tapaḥ ugram atiṣṭhanta — the Rākṣasas performed fierce austerity in the Puronuvāka; tān prajāpatiḥ vareṇa upāmantrayata — Prajāpati addressed them with a boon; tāni varam vṛṇīta — they chose a boon; ādityaḥ naḥ yoddhā hanti — “the Sun, our opponent, kills us”; tān prajāpatiḥ abravīt yodhayadhvam — Prajāpati said to them, “Fight”

prajāpatidatto varo'pi duḥkhaphalatvāt śāpa ucyate | tathā ca śrutiḥ-rakṣāṃsi ha vā puronuvāke tapogramatiṣṭhanta, tān prajāpatirvareṇopāmantrayata tāni varaṃ vṛṇītādityo no yoddhā hati tān prajāpatirabravīd yodhayadhvame ityādi

VP 2.8.52

ओङ्कारब्रह्मसंयुक्तं गायत्र्या चाभिमन्त्रितम् । तेन दह्यन्ति ते पापा वज्रीभूतेन वारिणा

That water becomes like a Vajraayudha, those sinners are burnt with that. With the mantra Gaayatri, it is made sacred. With the the Vyaahrutis and the Gaayatri mantra, braahmanaas give arghya during Sandhyaa kaala.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

With the the Vyaahrutis and the Gaayatri mantra, braahmanaas give arghya during Sandhyaa kaala.; tena dahyanti te pāpā vajrībhūtena vāriṇā — That water becomes like a Vajraayudha, those sinners are burnt with that. With the mantra Gaayatri, it is made sacred. With the the Vyaahrutis and the Gaayatri mantra, braahmanaas give arghya during Sandhyaa kaala.

dahyanti dahyante — they burn; they are burned; dvijaiḥ prakṣiptena vāriṇā — by the water cast by the twice-born

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

dahyanti dahyante | dvijaiḥ prakṣiptena vāriṇā

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

दह्यन्ति दह्यन्ते । द्विजैः प्रक्षिप्तेन वारिणा

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains that the demons are burned by the water offered by the twice-born during twilight worship. The mantra-charged water gives the Sun power to destroy them; therefore the twilight rite is obligatory.

VP 2.8.59

काष्ठा निमेषा दश पञ्च चैव त्रिंशच्च काष्ठा गणयेत्कलां च । त्रिंशत्कलश्चैव भवेन्मुहूर्तस्तैस्त्रिंशता रात्र्यहनी समेते

Now, Paraasharar is telling the division of time. This is to know the best time for performing yaagaas. 15 nimeshaas are said to be one kaashtha. 30 kaashthaas are one kalaa. One muhoorta is made of 30 kalaas. 30 muhoortaas are included in one day, inclusive of day and night. So, 15 muhoortaas during the day, and 15 muhoortaas in the night.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kāṣṭhā nimeṣā daśa pañca caiva triṃśacca kāṣṭhā gaṇayetkalāṃ ca — Now; triṃśatkalaścaiva bhavenmuhūrtastaistriṃśatā rātryahanī samete — Paraasharar is telling the division of time. This is to know the best time for performing yaagaas. 15 nimeshaas are said to be one kaashtha. 30 kaashthaas are one kalaa. One muhoorta is made of 30 kalaas. 30 muhoortaas are included in one day, inclusive of day and night. So, 15 muhoortaas during the day, and 15 muhoortaas in the night.

Śrīdhara

atha kālagaṇanāpūrvakaṃ sandhyākālanirṇayam āha—kāṣṭha iti dvābhyām — Sandhyaa kaala iyatta aajnyaapanartham aaha - kaashthaa iti | In order to know the duration of Sandhyaa kaala, the time divisions are told. At what time one has to do Sandhyaavandana, how much duration is Sandhyaa kaala - for all this, the divisions of time are to be known.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

kāṣṭhā nimeṣā daśa pañca caiva triṃśacca kāṣṭhā gaṇayetkalāṃ ca | triṃśatkalaścaiva bhavenmuhūrtastaistriṃśatā rātryahanī samete ||59||

atha kālagaṇanāpūrvakaṃ sandhyākālanirṇayam āha—kāṣṭha iti dvābhyām

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अथ कालगणनापूर्वकं सन्ध्याकालनिर्णयम् आह—काष्ठ इति द्वाभ्याम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Sandhyaa kaala iyatta aajnyaapanartham aaha - kaashthaa iti | In order to know the duration of Sandhyaa kaala, the time divisions are told. At what time one has to do Sandhyaavandana, how much duration is Sandhyaa kaala - for all this, the divisions of time are to be known.

VP 2.8.60

ह्रासवृद्धी त्वहर्भागोर्दिवसानां यथाक्रमम् । सन्ध्यामुहूर्तमात्रा वै ह्रासवृद्धौ समा स्मृता

The daytime is longer in the Uttaraayana and shorter in the Dakshinaayana; as was told earlier. The duration of day and night keeps changing - shortening and increasing. Whatever this be, the Sandhyaa kaala is said to be one muhoorta always, whether it is during the longer days or shorter days.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

hrāsavṛddhī tvaharbhāgordivasānāṃ yathākramam — The daytime is longer in the Uttaraayana and shorter in the Dakshinaayana; sandhyāmuhūrtamātrā vai hrāsavṛddhau samā smṛtā — as was told earlier. The duration of day and night keeps changing - shortening and increasing. Whatever this be, the Sandhyaa kaala is said to be one muhoorta always, whether it is during the longer days or shorter days.

Śrīdhara

ahar-bhāgaiḥ prātar-ādibhiḥ saha — together with the portions of the day beginning with morning; divasānāṃ hrāse vṛddhau ca — as the days decrease and increase; sandhyā tu muhūrta-mātrā — twilight is only one muhūrta; ardhodaya-pūrvaṃ prātaḥ sandhyā — morning twilight is before half-rise; sāyaṃ-sandhyā nāḍī-dvaya-pramāṇā — evening twilight measures two nāḍīs

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

hrāsavṛddhī tvaharbhāgordivasānāṃ yathākramam | sandhyāmuhūrtamātrā vai hrāsavṛddhau samā smṛtā ||60||

aharbhāgaiḥ prātarādibhiḥ saha divasānāṃ hrāse vṛddhau ca tadbhāgānām api hrāsavṛddhyoravaśyambhāvitvāt | sandhyā tu muhūrttamātrā arddhodayāt pūrvaṃ prātaḥ, sandhyā nāḍīdvayapramāṇā | ardhāstamayānantaraṃ sāyaṃsandhyā nāḍīdvayapramāṇaivetyarthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अहर्भागैः प्रातरादिभिः सह दिवसानां ह्रासे वृद्धौ च तद्भागानाम् अपि ह्रासवृद्ध्योरवश्यम्भावित्वात् । सन्ध्या तु मुहूर्त्तमात्रा अर्द्धोदयात् पूर्वं प्रातः, सन्ध्या नाडीद्वयप्रमाणा । अर्धास्तमयानन्तरं सायंसन्ध्या नाडीद्वयप्रमाणैवेत्यर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains the changing portions of the day. Morning twilight occurs before sunrise and evening twilight after sunset; each is measured by its prescribed period.

VP 2.8.61

रेखाप्रभृत्यथादित्ये त्रिमुहूर्तगते रवौ । प्रातः स्मृतस्ततः कालो भागश्चाह्नः स पञ्चमः

prātarādi-ahar-bhāgānāṃ vṛddhi-hrāsau — the increase and decrease of the portions of the day beginning with morning; vaiṣuvataṃ mānam — the equinoctial measure; ardhodayaḥ lekhā — the line is half-rise; pratyekaṃ muhūrta-trayeṇa — each portion consists of three muhūrtas; prātaḥ saṅgava-madhyāhna-aparāhna-sāyāhna — morning, forenoon, midday, afternoon, and evening

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

rekhāprabhṛtyathāditye trimuhūrtagate ravau | prātaḥ smṛtastataḥ kālo bhāgaścāhnaḥ sa pañcamaḥ ||61||

prātarādyaharbhāgānāṃ vṛddhihrāsau vaktuṃ teṣāṃ dhruvabhūtaṃ vaiṣuvataṃ mānaṃ tāvadāha—lekha iti sārddhaistribhiḥ | ardhodayo'tra lekhā tāmārabhya pratyekaṃ tu muhūrttatrayeṇa prātarādirekaikaḥ kālaḥ | tathā ca smṛtiḥ—muhūrttatritayaṃ prātastāvāneva ca saṅgavaḥ madhyāhrāstrimuhūrttaḥ syādaparāhṇo'pi tādṛśaḥ | sāyāhnastrimuhūrtaśca sarvakarmasu garhitaḥ | iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

प्रातराद्यहर्भागानां वृद्धिह्रासौ वक्तुं तेषां ध्रुवभूतं वैषुवतं मानं तावदाह—लेख इति सार्द्धैस्त्रिभिः । अर्धोदयोऽत्र लेखा तामारभ्य प्रत्येकं तु मुहूर्त्तत्रयेण प्रातरादिरेकैकः कालः । तथा च स्मृतिः—मुहूर्त्तत्रितयं प्रातस्तावानेव च सङ्गवः मध्याह्रास्त्रिमुहूर्त्तः स्यादपराह्णोऽपि तादृशः । सायाह्नस्त्रिमुहूर्तश्च सर्वकर्मसु गर्हितः । इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Aparaahne iti | Muhoortaah traya eva cha saayaahna iti poorvena anvayah | Idam cha dasha pancha muhoortaahe pancha dasha muhoorte ahni, praatah aadi panchaasha kalpanam iti sheshah | Dasha pancha muhoortaa vai iti cha paathah | Dasha pancha muhoortaah cha iti paathe vishuve pancha dasha dhruva bhootaa muhoortaah | Ayanoyoh tu vruddhi hraasa yogino muhoortaah traya eva iti sangraha uktih | Evening 3 muhoortaas is Saayaahna. 15 muhoortaas in a day, have to be counted starting from Praatah. In the Uttaraayana as well as Dakshinaayana, the muhoortaas are said to be three muhoortaas only (for Praatah kaala), but the day may be longer or shorter. In Vishuva, the day and night are equal, so muhoortaas also will become equal.

VP 2.8.62

तस्मात्प्रातस्तनात्कालात्त्रमुहूर्तस्तु सङ्गवः । मध्याह्नस्त्रिमुहूर्तस्तु तस्मात्कालात्तु सङ्गवात्

The next 3 muhootaas are said to be Saayaahna. With 15 muhoortaas in a day, each [[139]] part is 3 muhoortaas told. The day is divided into five parts - the first 3 muhoortaas are Praatah kaala; then comes Sangava kaala; then comes Madhyaahna; then Aparaahna; then Saayaahna.

The day is divided into five parts - the first 3 muhoortaas are Praatah kaala; madhyāhnastrimuhūrtastu tasmātkālāttu saṅgavāt — then comes Sangava kaala

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains the equinoctial measure of the day and how the divisions of day and night increase or decrease at other times.

शरद्वसन्तयोर्मध्ये विषुवं तु विभाव्यते । तुलामेषगते भानौ समरात्रिदिनं तु तत्

VP 2.8.68

कर्कटावस्थिते भानौ दक्षिणायनमुच्यते । उत्तरायणमप्युक्तं मकरस्थे दिवाकरे

VP 2.8.69

त्रिंशन्मुहूर्तं कथितमहोरात्रं तु यन्मया । तानिपञ्चदश ब्रह्मन् पक्ष इत्यभिधीयते

Whatever was told by Sri Paraasharar, 30 muhoortaas, in a day and night put together - 15 such days is said to be one paksha.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

triṃśanmuhūrtaṃ kathitamahorātraṃ tu yanmayā — Whatever was told by Sri Paraasharar, 30 muhoortaas, in a day and night put together - 15 such days is said to be one paksha.; tānipañcadaśa brahman pakṣa ityabhidhīyate — Whatever was told by Sri Paraasharar, 30 muhoortaas, in a day and night put together - 15 such days is said to be one paksha.

Śrīdhara

atha sūrya-gati-prasaṅgāt — then, in connection with the Sun's motion; tat-kṛtam saṃvatsara-ādi-pañcakam cakram āha — he states the cycle of five beginning with the year, produced by it; triṃśat iti sārdhaiḥ caturbhiḥ — in four and a half verses beginning with “thirty”; loka-prasiddhaiḥ sāvanair dina-pakṣa-māsaiḥ — by the commonly known civil days, fortnights, and months; arka-gati-janitaḥ dina-dayaḥ lakṣyante — the days produced by the Sun's motion are marked; ṛtu-ayana-ādi-abhidhānāt — through names such as season and solstice

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

triṃśanmuhūrtaṃ kathitamahorātraṃ tu yanmayā | tānipañcadaśa brahman pakṣa ityabhidhīyate ||69||

atha sūryagatiprasaṅgāt tatkṛtaṃ saṃvatsarādipañcakaṃ cakram āha—triṃśad iti sārdhaiścaturbhiḥ | lokaprasiddhaiḥ sāvanairdina-pakṣa-māsairaprasiddhā adūraviprakarṣādarkajā eva dinadayolakṣyante, arkagatyaiva ṛtvayanādyabhidhānāt

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अथ सूर्यगतिप्रसङ्गात् तत्कृतं संवत्सरादिपञ्चकं चक्रम् आह—त्रिंशद् इति सार्धैश्चतुर्भिः । लोकप्रसिद्धैः सावनैर्दिन-पक्ष-मासैरप्रसिद्धा अदूरविप्रकर्षादर्कजा एव दिनदयोलक्ष्यन्ते, अर्कगत्यैव ऋत्वयनाद्यभिधानात्

Śrīdhara commentary.

2 pakshaas form a month. 2 such months are said to be one rutu. 3 rutus is said to be one ayana. 2 ayanaas are called as one varsha.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

māsaḥ pakṣadvayenokto dvau māsau cārkajāvṛtuḥ | ṛtutrayaṃ cāpyayanaṃ dve'yane varṣasaṃjñite ||70||

atha sūryagatiprasaṅgāt tatkṛtaṃ saṃvatsarādipañcakaṃ cakram āha—triṃśad iti sārdhaiścaturbhiḥ | lokaprasiddhaiḥ sāvanairdina-pakṣa-māsairaprasiddhā adūraviprakarṣādarkajā eva dinadayolakṣyante, arkagatyaiva ṛtvayanādyabhidhānāt

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अथ सूर्यगतिप्रसङ्गात् तत्कृतं संवत्सरादिपञ्चकं चक्रम् आह—त्रिंशद् इति सार्धैश्चतुर्भिः । लोकप्रसिद्धैः सावनैर्दिन-पक्ष-मासैरप्रसिद्धा अदूरविप्रकर्षादर्कजा एव दिनदयोलक्ष्यन्ते, अर्कगत्यैव ऋत्वयनाद्यभिधानात्

Śrīdhara commentary.

2 pakshaas form a month. 2 such months are said to be one rutu. 3 rutus is said to be one ayana. 2 ayanaas are called as one varsha.

VP 2.8.71

संवत्सरादयः पञ्च चतुर्मासविकल्पिताः । निश्चयः सर्वकाल्सय युगमित्यभिधीयते

5 samvatsaraas is called as yuga, and is one kind of calculation. [[141]] SrI VishNu PurANam (Vol. 2)

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

saṃvatsarādayaḥ pañca caturmāsavikalpitāḥ — 5 samvatsaraas is called as yuga, and is one kind of calculation. [[141]] SrI VishNu PurANam (Vol. 2); niścayaḥ sarvakālsaya yugamityabhidhīyate — 5 samvatsaraas is called as yuga, and is one kind of calculation. [[141]] SrI VishNu PurANam (Vol. 2)

Śrīdhara

caturmāsa-vikalpitā iti — the year is considered through four kinds of months; darśādarśaḥ cāndraḥ — the lunar month is measured by new moon and full moon; triṃśad-divasaḥ sāvanaḥ — the civil month consists of thirty days; sauraḥ arka-rāśiḥ — the solar month is measured by the Sun’s passage through a zodiac sign; nākṣatraḥ cendu-maṇḍalataḥ — the stellar month is reckoned from the lunar sphere; ete caturvidhā māsāḥ — these are the four kinds of months

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

saṃvatsarādayaḥ pañca caturmāsavikalpitāḥ | niścayaḥ sarvakālsaya yugamityabhidhīyate ||71||

caturmāsavikalpitā iti | darśādarśaścāndraḥ triṃśaddivasastu sāvano māsaḥ | sauro'rkarāśiḥ nākṣatraścendumaṇḍalataḥ ॥ ityevaṃlakṣaṇāścāndrasāvanasauranākṣatraiścāturvidhairmāsairvividhatayā kalpitāḥ pañca saṃvatsarādayaḥ ekaṃ yugam | sāvanañcāpi saurañca cāndraṃ nākṣatram eva ca | catvāryetāni nāmāni yairyugaṃ pravibhajyate | iti vṛddhagaktiḥ | sarvakālasya malamāsādeniścayaḥ nirṇayahetuḥ | tathā hi—yadā śukle pratipadi ekasminnakṣatre candreṇa saha sthite sūryasaṅkrāntirbhavati, tadā caturvidhā māsā yugapat pravarttante | tathā ca sauramāse navavarṣe ṣaḍ dināni varddhante, hrasanti candramāsena ṣaḍ dināni | evaṃ candrārkayor vyavadhānatāratamyāt pañcavarṣātmake yuge saurāḥ ṣaṣṭirmāsāḥ, sāvanā ekaṣaṣṭiḥ, cāndrā dviṣaṣṭiḥ, nākṣatrāḥ saptaṣaṣṭiḥ | tanmadhye ca malamāsadvayaṃ bhavatītyevaṃ sarvakāniścayo bhavati | tataśca ṣaṣṭhe varṣe tathaiva candrārkayor yogādyugamityabhidhīyata ityarthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

चतुर्मासविकल्पिता इति । दर्शादर्शश्चान्द्रः त्रिंशद्दिवसस्तु सावनो मासः । सौरोऽर्कराशिः नाक्षत्रश्चेन्दुमण्डलतः ॥ इत्येवंलक्षणाश्चान्द्रसावनसौरनाक्षत्रैश्चातुर्विधैर्मासैर्विविधतया कल्पिताः पञ्च संवत्सरादयः एकं युगम् । सावनञ्चापि सौरञ्च चान्द्रं नाक्षत्रम् एव च । चत्वार्येतानि नामानि यैर्युगं प्रविभज्यते । इति वृद्धगक्तिः । सर्वकालस्य मलमासादेनिश्चयः निर्णयहेतुः । तथा हि—यदा शुक्ले प्रतिपदि एकस्मिन्नक्षत्रे चन्द्रेण सह स्थिते सूर्यसङ्क्रान्तिर्भवति, तदा चतुर्विधा मासा युगपत् प्रवर्त्तन्ते । तथा च सौरमासे नववर्षे षड् दिनानि वर्द्धन्ते, ह्रसन्ति चन्द्रमासेन षड् दिनानि । एवं चन्द्रार्कयोर् व्यवधानतारतम्यात् पञ्चवर्षात्मके युगे सौराः षष्टिर्मासाः, सावना एकषष्टिः, चान्द्रा द्विषष्टिः, नाक्षत्राः सप्तषष्टिः । तन्मध्ये च मलमासद्वयं भवतीत्येवं सर्वकानिश्चयो भवति । ततश्च षष्ठे वर्षे तथैव चन्द्रार्कयोर् योगाद्युगमित्यभिधीयत इत्यर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains the four kinds of month—lunar, civil, solar, and stellar—and why the proper times for rites and gifts must be known.

VP 2.8.72

संवत्सरस्तु प्रथमो द्वितीयः परिवत्सरः । इद्वत्सरस्तृतीयस्तु चतुर्थश्चानुवत्सरः । वत्सरः पञ्चमश्चात्र कालोयं युगसंज्ञितः

called Bálakhilyas, the sun goes on his course to give light to the world. Fifteen twinklings of the eye (Nimeshas) make a Kásht́há; thirty Kásht́hás, a Kalá;

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

saṃvatsarastu prathamo dvitīyaḥ parivatsaraḥ — called Bálakhilyas, the sun goes on his course to give light to the world. Fifteen twinklings of the eye (Nimeshas) make a Kásht́há; idvatsarastṛtīyastu caturthaścānuvatsaraḥ — thirty Kásht́hás, a Kalá;; vatsaraḥ pañcamaścātra kāloyaṃ yugasaṃjñitaḥ — thirty Kásht́hás, a Kalá;

Śrīdhara

caturmāsa-vikalpitā iti — the five years are distinguished through the four kinds of months; sāvanaṃ sauraṃ cāndraṃ nākṣatram eva ca — civil, solar, lunar, and stellar; catvāry etāni nāmāni — these are the four names; yair yugaṃ pravibhajyate — by which the yuga is divided; ekaṃ yugam — one yuga

Viṣṇu Purāṇa

saṃvatsarastu prathamo dvitīyaḥ parivatsaraḥ | idvatsarastṛtīyastu caturthaścānuvatsaraḥ | vatsaraḥ pañcamaścātra kāloyaṃ yugasaṃjñitaḥ ||72||

caturmāsavikalpitā iti | darśādarśaścāndraḥ triṃśaddivasastu sāvano māsaḥ | sauro'rkarāśiḥ nākṣatraścendumaṇḍalataḥ ॥ ityevaṃlakṣaṇāścāndrasāvanasauranākṣatraiścāturvidhairmāsairvividhatayā kalpitāḥ pañca saṃvatsarādayaḥ ekaṃ yugam | sāvanañcāpi saurañca cāndraṃ nākṣatram eva ca | catvāryetāni nāmāni yairyugaṃ pravibhajyate | iti vṛddhagaktiḥ | sarvakālasya malamāsādeniścayaḥ nirṇayahetuḥ | tathā hi—yadā śukle pratipadi ekasminnakṣatre candreṇa saha sthite sūryasaṅkrāntirbhavati, tadā caturvidhā māsā yugapat pravarttante | tathā ca sauramāse navavarṣe ṣaḍ dināni varddhante, hrasanti candramāsena ṣaḍ dināni | evaṃ candrārkayor vyavadhānatāratamyāt pañcavarṣātmake yuge saurāḥ ṣaṣṭirmāsāḥ, sāvanā ekaṣaṣṭiḥ, cāndrā dviṣaṣṭiḥ, nākṣatrāḥ saptaṣaṣṭiḥ | tanmadhye ca malamāsadvayaṃ bhavatītyevaṃ sarvakāniścayo bhavati | tataśca ṣaṣṭhe varṣe tathaiva candrārkayor yogādyugamityabhidhīyata ityarthaḥ

चतुर्मासविकल्पिता इति । दर्शादर्शश्चान्द्रः त्रिंशद्दिवसस्तु सावनो मासः । सौरोऽर्कराशिः नाक्षत्रश्चेन्दुमण्डलतः ॥ इत्येवंलक्षणाश्चान्द्रसावनसौरनाक्षत्रैश्चातुर्विधैर्मासैर्विविधतया कल्पिताः पञ्च संवत्सरादयः एकं युगम् । सावनञ्चापि सौरञ्च चान्द्रं नाक्षत्रम् एव च । चत्वार्येतानि नामानि यैर्युगं प्रविभज्यते । इति वृद्धगक्तिः । सर्वकालस्य मलमासादेनिश्चयः निर्णयहेतुः । तथा हि—यदा शुक्ले प्रतिपदि एकस्मिन्नक्षत्रे चन्द्रेण सह स्थिते सूर्यसङ्क्रान्तिर्भवति, तदा चतुर्विधा मासा युगपत् प्रवर्त्तन्ते । तथा च सौरमासे नववर्षे षड् दिनानि वर्द्धन्ते, ह्रसन्ति चन्द्रमासेन षड् दिनानि । एवं चन्द्रार्कयोर् व्यवधानतारतम्यात् पञ्चवर्षात्मके युगे सौराः षष्टिर्मासाः, सावना एकषष्टिः, चान्द्रा द्विषष्टिः, नाक्षत्राः सप्तषष्टिः । तन्मध्ये च मलमासद्वयं भवतीत्येवं सर्वकानिश्चयो भवति । ततश्च षष्ठे वर्षे तथैव चन्द्रार्कयोर् योगाद्युगमित्यभिधीयत इत्यर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains that a five-year cycle is determined by four kinds of month. Their differing measures are reconciled within the cycle called a yuga.

VP 2.8.73

यः श्वेतस्योत्तरः शैलः शृङ्गवानिति विश्रुतः । त्रीणि तस्य तु शृङ्गाणि मैरयं शृङ्गवान् स्मृतः

thirty Kalás, a Muhúrtta (forty-eight minutes); and thirty Muhúrttas, a day and night: the portions of the day are longer or shorter,

VP 2.8.76

प्रथमे कृत्तिकाभागे यदा भास्वांस्तदा शशी । विशाखानां चतुर्थेऽंशे मुने तिष्ठत्यसंशयम्

expiration of three Muhúrttas (two hours and twenty-four minutes), that interval is called Prátar (morning), forming a fifth portion

Śrīdhara

idānīṃ meṣākhyāṃ viṣuvākhyaṃ kālaṃ tatprāśastyaṃ cāha | prathama iti caturbhiḥ | prathame kṛttikābhāge meṣānte viśākhānāṃ caturthe aṃśe vṛścikopakrame tadā śaśī asaṃśayaṃ hi tiṣṭhati

VP 2.8.77

विशाखानां यदा सूर्यश्चरत्यंशं तृतीयकम् । तदा चन्द्रं विजानीयात्कृत्तिकाशिरसि स्थितम्

of the day. The next portion, or three Muhúrttas from morning, is termed Sangava (forenoon): the three next

Śrīdhara

yadā — when; candrārkayor api — of moon and sun also; ṣaḍrāśivyavadhāne — at a separation of six signs; viṣuvāvasthitarūpaṃ mahāviṣuvam — the great equinox, having the form of being situated at the equinox; viśākhānāṃ tṛtīyamaṃśam tu — the third part of Viśākhā; tulāntaram — the interval of Libra; kṛttikāśirasi — at the head of Kṛttikā; meṣānte — at the end of Aries; candraṃ — the moon; tadā vijānīyāt — then one should know

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

viśākhānāṃ yadā sūryaścaratyaṃśaṃ tṛtīyakam | tadā candraṃ vijānīyātkṛttikāśirasi sthitam ||77||

yadā candrārkayor api ṣaḍrāśivyavadhāne viṣuvāvasthitarūpaṃ mahāviṣuvaṃ viśākhānāṃ tṛtīyamaṃśantu tulāntaram | kṛttikāśirasi meṣānte candraṃ tadā vijānīyāt

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

यदा चन्द्रार्कयोर् अपि षड्राशिव्यवधाने विषुवावस्थितरूपं महाविषुवं विशाखानां तृतीयमंशन्तु तुलान्तरम् । कृत्तिकाशिरसि मेषान्ते चन्द्रं तदा विजानीयात्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: yadā candrārkayor api ṣaḍrāśivyavadhāne viṣuvāvasthitarūpaṃ mahāviṣuvaṃ viśākhānāṃ tṛtīyamaṃśantu tulāntaram | kṛttikāśirasi meṣānte candraṃ tadā vijānīyāt

VP 2.8.78

तदैव विषुवाख्योऽयं कालः पुण्योऽभिधीयते । तदा दानादि देयानि देवेभ्यः प्रयतात्मभिः

Muhúrttas constitute mid-day: the afternoon comprises the next three Muhúrttas: the three Muhúrttas following are

Viṣṇu Purāṇa

VP 2.8.79

ब्रह्मणेभ्यः पितृभ्यश्च मुखमेतत्तु दानाजम् । दत्तदानस्तु विषुवे कृतकृत्योभिजायते

considered as the evening: and the fifteen Muhúrttas of the day are thus classed in five portions of three each.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

brahmaṇebhyaḥ pitṛbhyaśca mukhametattu dānājam — considered as the evening: and the fifteen Muhúrttas of the day are thus classed in five portions of three each.; dattadānastu viṣuve kṛtakṛtyobhijāyate — considered as the evening: and the fifteen Muhúrttas of the day are thus classed in five portions of three each.

Śrīdhara

tasmāt ati-viśiṣṭam etat tu dāna-jam mukham — therefore this is supremely excellent, the mouth produced by giving; dāna-artha-jātam — born for the purpose of giving; vivṛtam devādīnām mukham — the open mouth of the gods and others; mukham etat tu daivatam — this indeed is the divine mouth; asmin kāle dattam sākṣāt devādīnām mukhe hutam bhavati — what is given at this time is directly offered into the mouth of the gods

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

brahmaṇebhyaḥ pitṛbhyaśca mukhametattu dānājam | dattadānastu viṣuve kṛtakṛtyobhijāyate ||79||

tasmādativiśiṣṭametattu dānajaṃ mukhaṃ dānārthajātaṃ vivṛtaṃ devādīnāṃ mukham | mukhametattu daivatam iti vāyūkteḥ | asmin kāle dattaṃ sākṣād devādīnāṃ mukhe hutaṃ bhavatītyarthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तस्मादतिविशिष्टमेतत्तु दानजं मुखं दानार्थजातं विवृतं देवादीनां मुखम् । मुखमेतत्तु दैवतम् इति वायूक्तेः । अस्मिन् काले दत्तं साक्षाद् देवादीनां मुखे हुतं भवतीत्यर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: tasmādativiśiṣṭametattu dānajaṃ mukhaṃ dānārthajātaṃ vivṛtaṃ devādīnāṃ mukham | mukhametattu daivatam iti vāyūkteḥ | asmin kāle dattaṃ sākṣād devādīnāṃ mukhe hutaṃ bhavatītyarthaḥ

VP 2.8.80

अहोरात्रार्धमासा तु कलाः काष्ठाः क्षणास्तथा । पौर्णमासी तथा ज्ञेया अमावास्या तथैव च । सिनीवाली कुहूश्चैव एका चानुमतिस्तथा

But the day consists of fifteen Muhúrttas only at the equinoxes, increasing or diminishing in number in the northern and southern declinations of the sun, when the day encroaches on the night,

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ahorātrārdhamāsā tu kalāḥ kāṣṭhāḥ kṣaṇāstathā — But the day consists of fifteen Muhúrttas only at the equinoxes, increasing or diminishing in number in the northern and southern declinations of the sun, when the day encroaches on the night,; paurṇamāsī tathā jñeyā amāvāsyā tathaiva ca — But the day consists of fifteen Muhúrttas only at the equinoxes, increasing or diminishing in number in the northern and southern declinations of the sun, when the day encroaches on the night,; sinīvālī kuhūścaiva ekā cānumatistathā — But the day consists of fifteen Muhúrttas only at the equinoxes, increasing or diminishing in number in the northern and southern declinations of the sun, when the day encroaches on the night,

Śrīdhara

puṇya-kāla-prasaṅgāt — in the context of auspicious times; yāga-ādi-puṇya-kāla-jñāpanārtham — to indicate auspicious times for sacrifice and the like; ahorātra-ādi-vibhāgaḥ samyak jñātavyaḥ — the divisions of day and night and so on should be properly known; dṛṣṭa-candrā sinīvālī — Sinīvālī has a visible moon; naṣṭa-candrā kuhūḥ — Kuhū has an invisible moon; ete amāvāsyā-bhedau — these are the two forms of new moon; pūrṇa-candrā rākā — Rākā has a full moon; kalā-hīna-candraḥ anumatiḥ — Anumati has a moon lacking a digit; iti paurṇamāsī-bhedau — these are the two forms of full moon

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ahorātrārdhamāsā tu kalāḥ kāṣṭhāḥ kṣaṇāstathā | paurṇamāsī tathā jñeyā amāvāsyā tathaiva ca | sinīvālī kuhūścaiva ekā cānumatistathā ||80||

puṇyakālaprasaṅgāt yāgādipuṇyakālajñāpanārtham ahorātrādivibhāgaḥ samyak jñātavya ityāha | ahorātreti sārddhena | dṛṣṭacandrā sinivālī, naṣṭacandrā kuhūḥ ityetāvamāvāsyābhedau | pūrṇacandrā rākā kalāhīnacandrānumatiriti paurṇamāsī bhedau

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

पुण्यकालप्रसङ्गात् यागादिपुण्यकालज्ञापनार्थम् अहोरात्रादिविभागः सम्यक् ज्ञातव्य इत्याह । अहोरात्रेति सार्द्धेन । दृष्टचन्द्रा सिनिवाली, नष्टचन्द्रा कुहूः इत्येतावमावास्याभेदौ । पूर्णचन्द्रा राका कलाहीनचन्द्रानुमतिरिति पौर्णमासी भेदौ

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: puṇyakālaprasaṅgāt yāgādipuṇyakālajñāpanārtham ahorātrādivibhāgaḥ samyak jñātavya ityāha | ahorātreti sārddhena | dṛṣṭacandrā sinivālī, naṣṭacandrā kuhūḥ ityetāvamāvāsyābhedau | pūrṇacandrā rākā kalāhīnacandrānumatiriti paurṇamāsī bhedau

VP 2.8.81

तपस्तपस्यै मधुमाधवौ च शुक्रः शुचिश्चायनमुत्तरं स्यात् । नभोनभस्यौ च इषस्तथोर्जःसहः सहस्याविति दक्षिणं तत्

These are the names appearing in the Shruti, Vedaas, and is counted with Maagha maasa as the first month. Tapas, Tapasya, Madhu, Maadhava, Shukra and Shuchi are said to be Uttaraayana. Nabha, Nabhasya, Isha, Oorja, Saha, Sahasya are Dakshinaayana. Tapa and Tapasya are Maagha and Phaalguna. Madhu and Maadhava are Chaitra and Vaishaakha. Shukra and Shuchi are Jyeshtha and Aashaadha. These six months are Uttaraayana. Nabha and Nabhasya are Shraavana and Bhaadrapaada. Isha and Oor ja are Aashvina and Kaartika. Saha and Sahasya are Maargashira (Agrahaayana) and Pausha.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

tapastapasyai madhumādhavau ca śukraḥ śuciścāyanamuttaraṃ syāt — These are the names appearing in the Shruti; nabhonabhasyau ca iṣastathorjaḥsahaḥ sahasyāviti dakṣiṇaṃ tat — Vedaas, and is counted with Maagha maasa as the first month. Tapas

Śrīdhara

ṛtutrayaṃ cāpyayanam ity anena uktam — and the three seasons are called a course, it was said by this; ayanadvayaṃ vivicyāha — distinguishing the two courses, he says; tapa iti — “tapa,” thus; māghādidvidvimābhiḥ — by two-month periods beginning with Māgha; ṛtutrayam uttarāyaṇam — three seasons [constitute] the northern course; śrāvaṇādidvidvimāsaiḥ — by two-month periods beginning with Śrāvaṇa; ṛtutrayaṃ dakṣiṇāyanam — three seasons [constitute] the southern course; ityarthaḥ — this is the meaning; etacca sthūladṛṣṭyaivoktam — this too is stated only from a broad view

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

tapastapasyai madhumādhavau ca śukraḥ śuciścāyanamuttaraṃ syāt | nabhonabhasyau ca iṣastathorjaḥsahaḥ sahasyāviti dakṣiṇaṃ tat ||81||

ṛtutrayaṃ cāpyayanamityanannoktamayanadvayaṃ vivicyāha—tapa iti | māghādidvidvimānairṛtutrayam uttarāyaṇam | śrāvaṇādidvidvimāsairṛtutrayaṃ dakṣiṇāyanamityarthaḥ | etacca sthūladṛṣṭyaivoktam

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ऋतुत्रयं चाप्ययनमित्यनन्नोक्तमयनद्वयं विविच्याह—तप इति । माघादिद्विद्विमानैरृतुत्रयम् उत्तरायणम् । श्रावणादिद्विद्विमासैरृतुत्रयं दक्षिणायनमित्यर्थः । एतच्च स्थूलदृष्ट्यैवोक्तम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Iti varaahamira ukti lingaat | In olden times, it was done starting from Maagha. Starting from Dhanishtha is Uttaraayana, and starting from Aashlesha is Dakshinaayana. Perhaps it was like that in some shaastraas. Now, it is Mrugashira and Karkataka. Varaahamihira has told like this. Having told about the knowledge of Ayanaas, Vishuva, divisions of time, that one has to know this for giving charity, good deeds, yaagaas, now, incidentally, Paraasharar is telling about the four Lokapaalaas here.

VP 2.8.82

लोका लोकश्च यः शैलः प्रागुक्तो भवतो मया । लोकपालास्तु चत्वारस्तत्र तिष्ठन्ति सुव्रताः

I had told earlier about Lokaaloka parvata. There, four Lokapaalaas are present.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

lokā lokaśca yaḥ śailaḥ prāgukto bhavato mayā — I had told earlier about Lokaaloka parvata. There, four Lokapaalaas are present.; lokapālāstu catvārastatra tiṣṭhanti suvratāḥ — I had told earlier about Lokaaloka parvata. There, four Lokapaalaas are present.

Śrīdhara

lokāloka-uttara-śṛṅgāt agastya-sthānāt pitṛ-yānādikam kathayiṣyan — intending to describe the ancestral path and the rest from Lokāloka's northern peak and Agastya's station; prathamam tāvat buddhi-sthānam lokāloka-anuloka-pālān āha — first he states the guardians of Lokāloka, a place for understanding; lokāloka iti tribhiḥ — in three verses beginning “Lokāloka”; lokapālāḥ sthitāḥ loke lokāloke caturdiśam — the guardians of the worlds stand in the four directions on Lokāloka; uttaraṃ yat agastya-sya śṛṅgam deva-ṛṣi-pūjitam — north is the peak of Agastya, worshipped by divine sages

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

lokā lokaśca yaḥ śailaḥ prāgukto bhavato mayā | lokapālāstu catvārastatra tiṣṭhanti suvratāḥ ||82||

lokālokottaraśṛṅgādagastyasthānāt pitṛyānādikaṃ kathayiṣyan prathamaṃ tāvadbuddhisthān lokalokasyānulokapālānāha lokāloka iti tribhiḥ | taduktaṃ mātsye— lokapālāḥ sthitā hote lokāloke caturdiśam | uttaraṃ yadagastyasya śṛṅgaṃ devarṣipūjitam iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

लोकालोकोत्तरशृङ्गादगस्त्यस्थानात् पितृयानादिकं कथयिष्यन् प्रथमं तावद्बुद्धिस्थान् लोकलोकस्यानुलोकपालानाह लोकालोक इति त्रिभिः । तदुक्तं मात्स्ये— लोकपालाः स्थिता होते लोकालोके चतुर्दिशम् । उत्तरं यदगस्त्यस्य शृङ्गं देवर्षिपूजितम् इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Kardama's sons Sudhaama and Shankapaat, and Hiranyaroma and Ketumaan are the Lokapaalaas.

VP 2.8.84

निर्धन्द्वा निरभीमाना निस्तन्द्रा निष्परिग्रहाः । लोकपालाः स्थिता ह्येते लोका लोके चतुर्दिशम्

All the four directions, these Lokapaalaas are protecting and looking after these areas. They don't have desire-hatred and such dualities. They don't have abhimaana, egoism. They are never lazy; they don't sleep at all, are always awake. They are performing their duty in a selfless way.

They don't have abhimaana, egoism. They are never lazy; lokapālāḥ sthitā hyete lokā loke caturdiśam — they don't sleep at all, are always awake. They are performing their duty in a selfless way.

VP 2.8.85

उत्तरं पदगस्त्यस्य अजवीथ्याश्च दक्षिणम् । पितृयानः स वै पन्था वैश्वानरपथाद्बाहिः

[[148]] Nine veethis are told. To the north of Agastya's place, and to the south of Ajaveethi, this is said to be the Pitruyaana path. This does not include the Vaishvaanara veethi. Outside of Vaishvaanara, and to the north of Agastya, to the south of Ajaveethi, is said to be Pitruyaana. 27 nakshatraas are divided into nine, having three each, known as veethis. To the north is Naagaveethi, Gajaveethi and Airaavathee. In the middle are Vaarshabhiveethi, Goveethi and Jaradgavaveethi. To the south are Ajaveethi, Mrugaveethi and Vaishvaanaraveethi. Out of these, Mrugaveethi is said to be Pitruyaana, where Pitrus live. Though Vaishvaanaraveethi is south of Agastya, it is included in Devayaana, and is going to be told.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

uttaraṃ padagastyasya ajavīthyāśca dakṣiṇam — [[148]] Nine veethis are told. To the north of Agastya's place, and to the south of Ajaveethi, this is said to be the Pitruyaana path. This does not include the Vaishvaanara veethi. Outside of Vaishvaanara, and to the north of Agastya, to the south of Ajaveethi, is said to be Pitruyaana. 27 nakshatraas are divided into nine, having three each, known as veethis. To the north is Naagaveethi; pitṛyānaḥ sa vai panthā vaiśvānarapathādbāhiḥ — Gajaveethi and Airaavathee. In the middle are Vaarshabhiveethi

Śrīdhara

tatra pitṛyānam āha — here he explains the Pitṛyāna; jyotiścakre vīthī-kramaḥ — the order of the celestial courses; sarva-grahāṇāṃ trīṇi sthānāni — there are three positions for all the planets; uttaraṃ madhyaṃ dakṣiṇam — northern, middle, and southern; mṛgavīthī-mātraṃ pitṛyānam — the Pitṛyāna is only the Mṛgavīthī; vaiśvānarapathād bahiḥ — outside the Vaiśvānara path

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

uttaraṃ padagastyasya ajavīthyāśca dakṣiṇam | pitṛyānaḥ sa vai panthā vaiśvānarapathādbāhiḥ ||85||

tatra pitṛyānam āha—uttaram iti sārddhaiḥ pañcabhiḥ | tatra jyotiścakre vīthīkramo vāyunoktaḥ— sarvagrahāṇāṃ trīṇyeva sthānāni dvijasattama | sthānaṃ jārodravaṃ madhyaṃ tathairāvatamuttaram | vaiśvānaraṃ dakṣiṇato nirdiṣṭamiha tattvataḥ ॥ tad evamadhyamottaradakṣiṇamārgatrayaṃ pratyekaṃ vīthītrayeṇa tridhā bhidyate | tathāhi | tribhistribhiraścinyādinakṣatrairnāgavīthī gajavīthī airāvatī cety uttaramārge vīthītrayam | ārṣabhāge vīthī jārodgavī ceti vaiṣuvatamadhyamamārge vīthītrayam | ajavīthī mṛgavīthī vaiśvānarī ceti | dakṣiṇamārge vīthītrayam | taduktaṃ tatraiva— aśvinī kṛttikā yāmyā nāgavīthīti śabditā | rohiṇyārdrā mṛgaśiro gajavīthyabhidhīyate | puṣyāśleṣā tathādityā vīthī cairāvatī smṛtā | etāstu vīthayastisra uttaro mārga ucyate | tathā dve cāpi phalgunyau maghā caivārṣabhīmatā | hastā citrā tathā svātī govīthīti tu śabditā | jyeṣṭhā viśākhānurādhā vīthī jāradgavī matā | etāstu vīthayastisro madhyamo mārga ucyate | mūlāṣāḍhottarāṣāḍhā ajavīthyabhiśabditā | śravaṇā ca dhaniṣṭhā ca mārgo śatabhiṣak tathā | vaiśānarī bhādrapade revatī caiva kīrtitā | etāstu vīthayastisro dakṣiṇo mārga ucyate | iti ॥ yāmyā bharaṇī ādityā aditidevatākā punarvasuḥ | mārgī mṛgavīthī evaṃ sthite āgastyāduttaramajavīthyāśca dakṣiṇam | agastyasya nikaṭavartino vaiśvānarapathād bahiḥ | vaiśvānaravīthīṃ varjayitvā mṛgavīthīmātraṃ pitṛyānamityarthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत्र पितृयानम् आह—उत्तरम् इति सार्द्धैः पञ्चभिः । तत्र ज्योतिश्चक्रे वीथीक्रमो वायुनोक्तः— सर्वग्रहाणां त्रीण्येव स्थानानि द्विजसत्तम । स्थानं जारोद्रवं मध्यं तथैरावतमुत्तरम् । वैश्वानरं दक्षिणतो निर्दिष्टमिह तत्त्वतः ॥ तद् एवमध्यमोत्तरदक्षिणमार्गत्रयं प्रत्येकं वीथीत्रयेण त्रिधा भिद्यते । तथाहि । त्रिभिस्त्रिभिरश्चिन्यादिनक्षत्रैर्नागवीथी गजवीथी ऐरावती चेत्य् उत्तरमार्गे वीथीत्रयम् । आर्षभागे वीथी जारोद्गवी चेति वैषुवतमध्यममार्गे वीथीत्रयम् । अजवीथी मृगवीथी वैश्वानरी चेति । दक्षिणमार्गे वीथीत्रयम् । तदुक्तं तत्रैव— अश्विनी कृत्तिका याम्या नागवीथीति शब्दिता । रोहिण्यार्द्रा मृगशिरो गजवीथ्यभिधीयते । पुष्याश्लेषा तथादित्या वीथी चैरावती स्मृता । एतास्तु वीथयस्तिस्र उत्तरो मार्ग उच्यते । तथा द्वे चापि फल्गुन्यौ मघा चैवार्षभीमता । हस्ता चित्रा तथा स्वाती गोवीथीति तु शब्दिता । ज्येष्ठा विशाखानुराधा वीथी जारद्गवी मता । एतास्तु वीथयस्तिस्रो मध्यमो मार्ग उच्यते । मूलाषाढोत्तराषाढा अजवीथ्यभिशब्दिता । श्रवणा च धनिष्ठा च मार्गो शतभिषक् तथा । वैशानरी भाद्रपदे रेवती चैव कीर्तिता । एतास्तु वीथयस्तिस्रो दक्षिणो मार्ग उच्यते । इति ॥ याम्या भरणी आदित्या अदितिदेवताका पुनर्वसुः । मार्गी मृगवीथी एवं स्थिते आगस्त्यादुत्तरमजवीथ्याश्च दक्षिणम् । अगस्त्यस्य निकटवर्तिनो वैश्वानरपथाद् बहिः । वैश्वानरवीथीं वर्जयित्वा मृगवीथीमात्रं पितृयानमित्यर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary classifies the celestial courses into northern, middle, and southern divisions, each with three paths. The Pitṛyāna is identified with the Mṛgavīthī outside the Vaiśvānara path.

VP 2.8.86

VP 2.8.88

जायमानास्तु पूर्वे च पश्चिमानां गृहेषु वै । पश्चिमाश्चैव पूर्वेषां जायन्ते विधनेष्विह

The fathers are reborn in their children's families, and children are again reborn in their forefather's kula. The progeny continues like this.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

jāyamānāstu pūrve ca paścimānāṃ gṛheṣu vai — The fathers are reborn in their children's families, and children are again reborn in their forefather's kula. The progeny continues like this.; paścimāścaiva pūrveṣāṃ jāyante vidhaneṣviha — The fathers are reborn in their children's families, and children are again reborn in their forefather's kula. The progeny continues like this.

Śrīdhara

santatyā vaṃśa-pravartanena — through continuation of lineage; maryādābhiḥ varṇāśrama-vyavasthābhiḥ — through the established order of varṇa and āśrama; śrutena śāstra-pravartanena ca — and through scripture and the transmission of sacred teaching; pūrvāḥ pitaraḥ paścimānāṃ putrāṇāṃ — earlier fathers become the fathers of later sons; paścimāḥ putrāḥ pūrveṣāṃ pitṝṇām — later sons become the fathers of earlier ancestors; svādhikāra-vaśāt jāyante — they arise according to their respective roles

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

santatyā vaṃśapravarttanena maryādābhiḥ varṇāśramādi-vyavasthābhiḥ, śrunena śāstrapravarttanena ca pūrve pitaraḥ paścimānāṃ putrāṇāṃ, paścimāḥ putrāśca pūrveṣāṃ svaputratvenotpannānāṃ pitṝṇāṃ nidhaneṣu gṛheṣu kenacidaṃśena svādhikāravaśāt jāyante | pitā putreṇa pitṛmānyo viyonau' iti śruteḥ | tathā ca vāyuḥ | prāpte tretāyuge caiva punaḥ saptaśayastyiha | pravarttayanti nān varṇānāśramāṃśca pṛthak-pṛthak | teṣāmevānnaye dhīrā utpadyante punaḥ punaḥ | jāyamānaḥ pitā putre putraḥ pitari caiva hi ॥ evamāvarttamānāste dvāpareṣu punaḥ punaḥ | kalpānāṃ bhāṣyavidyānāṃ jñānaśāstrakṛtaśca ye iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

सन्तत्या वंशप्रवर्त्तनेन मर्यादाभिः वर्णाश्रमादि-व्यवस्थाभिः, श्रुनेन शास्त्रप्रवर्त्तनेन च पूर्वे पितरः पश्चिमानां पुत्राणां, पश्चिमाः पुत्राश्च पूर्वेषां स्वपुत्रत्वेनोत्पन्नानां पितॄणां निधनेषु गृहेषु केनचिदंशेन स्वाधिकारवशात् जायन्ते । पिता पुत्रेण पितृमान्यो वियोनौऽ इति श्रुतेः । तथा च वायुः । प्राप्ते त्रेतायुगे चैव पुनः सप्तशयस्त्यिह । प्रवर्त्तयन्ति नान् वर्णानाश्रमांश्च पृथक्-पृथक् । तेषामेवान्नये धीरा उत्पद्यन्ते पुनः पुनः । जायमानः पिता पुत्रे पुत्रः पितरि चैव हि ॥ एवमावर्त्तमानास्ते द्वापरेषु पुनः पुनः । कल्पानां भाष्यविद्यानां ज्ञानशास्त्रकृतश्च ये इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Oordhvaretaas are rishis who have controlled their desires, kaama. There are 88,000 of them. They are present in the north part of the Sun. They are present till the Naimittika pralaya.

VP 2.8.92

अष्टाशीतिसहस्राणि मुनीनामूर्ध्वरेत साम् । उदक्पन्थनमर्यम्णः स्थिता ह्याभूतसंप्लवम्

sun is in the third degree of Viśákhá, and the moon is in the head of Krittiká

Śrīdhara

cāryyamṇaḥ saviturudak panthānam uttaraṃ mārgaṃ sthitāḥ śritāni | ākāraśchāndasaḥ — Śrīdhara gloss: cāryyamṇaḥ saviturudak panthānam uttaraṃ mārgaṃ sthitāḥ śritāni | ākāraśchāndasaḥ

VP 2.8.93

ते सेप्रयोगाल्लोभस्य मैथुनस्य च वर्जनात् । इच्छाद्वेषाप्रवृत्त्या च भूतारम्भवि वर्जनात्

Because they have given up stree sanga, lobha, they don't have icchaa, dvesha, and the dualities, they are not interested in procreation.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

te seprayogāllobhasya maithunasya ca varjanāt — Because they have given up stree sanga, lobha, they don't have icchaa, dvesha, and the dualities, they are not interested in procreation.; icchādveṣāpravṛttyā ca bhūtārambhavi varjanāt — Because they have given up stree sanga, lobha, they don't have icchaa, dvesha, and the dualities, they are not interested in procreation.

Śrīdhara

saptarṣibhyaḥ dakṣiṇataḥ devayānam — the Devayāna is to the south of the Seven Sages; tad ūrdhvaṃ viṣṇu-padasthitiḥ — above that is the position of Viṣṇupada; ūrdhvaṃ tad-upari — higher, above it; dhruvaḥ yatra tiṣṭhati — where Dhruva stands; viṣṇu-pada-ākhyam tṛtīyam sthānam — the third place called Viṣṇupada; bhūmi-apekṣayā divyam — divine in relation to the earth

Śrīdhara

uttarasyāṃ diśy ūrdhvaṃ yatra dhruvas tiṣṭhati, tad dhruvasyāśraya-bhūtaṃ viṣṇu-padākhyaṃ bhūmy-apekṣayā divyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ sthānam ity arthaḥ — They have seen defects in all the objects of senses like shabda, sparsha, roopa, rasa, gandha. They re never interested in kaama, stree sanga, procreation. Because of all these reasons, being very pure, they have attained amrutatva.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

te seprayogāllobhasya maithunasya ca varjanāt | icchādveṣāpravṛttyā ca bhūtārambhavi varjanāt ||93||

saptarṣibhyo dakṣiṇato devayānantviti uktam | tadūrdhvāntarato viṣṇupadasthitimāha urddhveti yāvat samāptiḥ | ūrdhvaṃ tadupari taduttarañca | saptarṣibhyaḥ uttarasyāṃ diśyūrddhvaṃ yatra dhruvastiṣṭhati taddhuvasyāśrayabhūtaṃ viṣṇupadākhyaṃ bhūmyapekṣayādivyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ sthānamityarthaḥ śrīdharaḥ: uttarasyāṃ diśy ūrdhvaṃ yatra dhruvas tiṣṭhati, tad dhruvasyāśraya-bhūtaṃ viṣṇu-padākhyaṃ bhūmy-apekṣayā divyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ sthānam ity arthaḥ

uttarasyāṃ diśy ūrdhvaṃ yatra dhruvas tiṣṭhati, tad dhruvasyāśraya-bhūtaṃ viṣṇu-padākhyaṃ bhūmy-apekṣayā divyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ sthānam ity arthaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

सप्तर्षिभ्यो दक्षिणतो देवयानन्त्विति उक्तम् । तदूर्ध्वान्तरतो विष्णुपदस्थितिमाह उर्द्ध्वेति यावत् समाप्तिः । ऊर्ध्वं तदुपरि तदुत्तरञ्च । सप्तर्षिभ्यः उत्तरस्यां दिश्यूर्द्ध्वं यत्र ध्रुवस्तिष्ठति तद्धुवस्याश्रयभूतं विष्णुपदाख्यं भूम्यपेक्षयादिव्यं तृतीयं स्थानमित्यर्थः ॥९३॥ श्रीधरः: उत्तरस्यां दिश्य् ऊर्ध्वं यत्र ध्रुवस् तिष्ठति, तद् ध्रुवस्याश्रय-भूतं विष्णु-पदाख्यं भूम्य्-अपेक्षया दिव्यं तृतीयं स्थानम् इत्य् अर्थः

उत्तरस्यां दिश्य् ऊर्ध्वं यत्र ध्रुवस् तिष्ठति, तद् ध्रुवस्याश्रय-भूतं विष्णु-पदाख्यं भूम्य्-अपेक्षया दिव्यं तृतीयं स्थानम् इत्य् अर्थः

Śrīdhara commentary.

They have seen defects in all the objects of senses like shabda, sparsha, roopa, rasa, gandha. They re never interested in kaama, stree sanga, procreation. Because of all these reasons, being very pure, they have attained amrutatva. They have seen defects in all the objects of senses like shabda, sparsha, roopa, rasa, gandha. They re never interested in kaama, stree sanga, procreation. Because of all these reasons, being very pure, they have attained amrutatva.

VP 2.8.95

आभूतसंप्लवं स्थानममृतत्वं विभाव्यते । त्रैलोक्यस्थितिकालोयमपुनर्मार उच्यते

to be made to the Brahmans by serious persons; for such donations are productive of happiness. Liberality at

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ābhūtasaṃplavaṃ sthānamamṛtatvaṃ vibhāvyate — to be made to the Brahmans by serious persons; trailokyasthitikāloyamapunarmāra ucyate — for such donations are productive of happiness. Liberality at

Śrīdhara

kṣīṇā aśeṣārter nānā-deha-prāpter hetavo yeṣāṃ te — Aabhoota samplavam brahma dinaantam yaavat sthiteh amrutatva aakhyaanam | Tat udak maargasthaanaam muktih iti aaha aabhoota samplavam sthaanam amrutatvam iti | Trailokya sthiti kaala eva iti amruta apunarmaara paryaayah | Naimittika pralaya is the end of the day of Chaturmukha Brahma, one kalpa. Praakruta pralaya is with 100 years of Chaturmukha Brahma, when laya of Chaturmukha Brahma also happens, when entire prakruti [[152]] mandala merges. These rishis live upto the Naimittika pralaya. Because they are present till the day of Chaturmukha Brahma, one kalpa, this is said to be amrutatva. This is the duration for which the three worlds - Bhooh, Bhuvah, Suvah exist; at the end of this, these three worlds merge, get destroyed; this is said to be Aabhoota Sambhava. This is said to be the mukti of those in the northern part of Sun, this is told because they live as long as the three worlds are present. At the pralaya which happens to the three worlds at the end of a day of Chaturmukha Brahma, they also get merged.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ābhūtasaṃplavaṃ sthānamamṛtatvaṃ vibhāvyate | trailokyasthitikāloyamapunarmāra ucyate ||95||

kṣīṇā aśeṣārter nānā-deha-prāpter hetavo yeṣāṃ te

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

क्षीणा अशेषार्तेर् नाना-देह-प्राप्तेर् हेतवो येषां ते

Śrīdhara commentary.

Aabhoota samplavam brahma dinaantam yaavat sthiteh amrutatva aakhyaanam | Tat udak maargasthaanaam muktih iti aaha aabhoota samplavam sthaanam amrutatvam iti | Trailokya sthiti kaala eva iti amruta apunarmaara paryaayah | Naimittika pralaya is the end of the day of Chaturmukha Brahma, one kalpa. Praakruta pralaya is with 100 years of Chaturmukha Brahma, when laya of Chaturmukha Brahma also happens, when entire prakruti [[152]] mandala merges. These rishis live upto the Naimittika pralaya. Because they are present till the day of Chaturmukha Brahma, one kalpa, this is said to be amrutatva. This is the duration for which the three worlds - Bhooh, Bhuvah, Suvah exist; at the end of this, these three worlds merge, get destroyed; this is said to be Aabhoota Sambhava. This is said to be the mukti of those in the northern part of Sun, this is told because they live as long as the three worlds are present. At the pralaya which happens to the three worlds at the end of a day of Chaturmukha Brahma, they also get merged.

VP 2.8.96

ब्रह्महत्याश्वमेधाभ्यां पापपुण्यकृतो विधिः । आभूतसंप्लवान्तन्तु फलमुक्तं तयोर्द्विज

Those who do Brahma hatyaa, a mahaa paapa; and those who perform Ashvamedha Yaaga, which is a punya; the fruits, paapa and punya respectively, are said to be present till the end of this kalpa. For one who does brahma hatyaa, his paapa will not get destroyed for such a long time. Those who do Ashvamedha yaaga, their punya makes them live for a long time.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

brahmahatyāśvamedhābhyāṃ pāpapuṇyakṛto vidhiḥ — Those who do Brahma hatyaa, a mahaa paapa; ābhūtasaṃplavāntantu phalamuktaṃ tayordvija — and those who perform Ashvamedha Yaaga, which is a punya

Śrīdhara

tat sāmyaāt tena viṣṇunā samāna-ācārya-yatnena — through equality, by effort equal to Viṣṇu's; indriya-ādi-vaśī-kāra-sāmarthyena utpannaḥ yaḥ yogaḥ — the yoga produced by mastery of the senses and the rest; sarvaiḥ tena iddhāḥ dīptāḥ — by it all are kindled and shining; ācārya-yati-ād vihitaḥ yogaḥ — yoga prescribed by teacher and ascetic; yogāt ācārya-yatitam iṣyate — from yoga, being made to strive is intended; kathaṃcit na upapadyeta — it would not arise in any other way

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

brahmahatyāśvamedhābhyāṃ pāpapuṇyakṛto vidhiḥ | ābhūtasaṃplavāntantu phalamuktaṃ tayordvija ||96||

tat sāmyāt tena viṣṇunā samānācāryayatieṇa indriyādi-vaśīkāra-sāmarthyenotpanno yo yogaḥ sarvhiḥ, teneddhā dīptāḥ | ācāryayatiād vihito yogo yogād ācāryayatiam iṣyate | kathañcin nopapadyeta iti harivaṃśokteḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत् साम्यात् तेन विष्णुना समानाचार्ययतिएण इन्द्रियादि-वशीकार-सामर्थ्येनोत्पन्नो यो योगः सर्व्हिः, तेनेद्धा दीप्ताः । आचार्ययतिआद् विहितो योगो योगाद् आचार्ययतिअम् इष्यते । कथञ्चिन् नोपपद्येत इति हरिवंशोक्तेः

Śrīdhara commentary.

Brahma hatyaa ashvamedhabhyaam iti | Brahma hatyaa ashvamedhabhyaam kriyaabhyaam paapa punya apoorve bhaavayatah pumso ayam phala bhogo vidhih | Evam aakalpam brahmahaa narakam ashvamedhee svargam bhunkta iti arthah | Apoorva is what comes out of a yajnya, a fruit. Those who do these brahma hatyaa or Ashvamedha yaaga, their fruits are said to be this. One who does Brahma hatyaa, he will suffer in naraka till the end of the kalpa. One who performs Ashvamedha yaaga, he enjoys svarga till end of the kalpa.

VP 2.8.97

यावन्मात्रे प्रदेशे तु मैत्रेयावस्थितो ध्रुवः । क्षयमायाति तावत्तु भूमेराभूतसंप्लवात्

All the worlds which are present till the Dhruva's place, only those places are destroyed - these three worlds. Aabhoota sambhava is naimittika pralaya; the three worlds will get destroyed during naimittika pralaya.

Aabhoota sambhava is naimittika pralaya; kṣayamāyāti tāvattu bhūmerābhūtasaṃplavāt — the three worlds will get destroyed during naimittika pralaya.

Transliteration

No commentary.

VP 2.8.98

ऊर्ध्वोत्तरमृषिभ्यस्तु ध्रुवो यत्र व्यवस्थितः । एतद्विष्णुपदं दिव्यं तृतीयं व्योम्नि भासुरम्

Beyond these three worlds, and where Dhruva is also present, this is said to be Vishnu pada, which is very bright, shining, full of light. This is said to be the third place. Above that, where Dhruva is there, rishis live there. This is Vishnu Pada, the third world with respect to Pruthivi.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ūrdhvottaramṛṣibhyastu dhruvo yatra vyavasthitaḥ — Beyond these three worlds, and where Dhruva is also present, this is said to be Vishnu pada, which is very bright, shining, full of light. This is said to be the third place. Above that, where Dhruva is there, rishis live there. This is Vishnu Pada, the third world with respect to Pruthivi.; etadviṣṇupadaṃ divyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ vyomni bhāsuram — Beyond these three worlds, and where Dhruva is also present, this is said to be Vishnu pada, which is very bright, shining, full of light. This is said to be the third place. Above that, where Dhruva is there, rishis live there. This is Vishnu Pada, the third world with respect to Pruthivi.

Śrīdhara

divi ātataṃ — spread through heaven; sarva-prakāśam — illuminating all; sūrya-rūpaṃ cakṣur iva mahātmanām — like an eye in the form of the Sun for the great souls; vitataṃ sarva-bhāsakatvena — extended because it illuminates everything; viveka-jñānena prapañca-vailakṣaṇya-jñānena vṛddham — expanded through discriminative knowledge of the distinction from the world; aparicchinnaṃ jñānam — knowledge without limitation; iti arthaḥ — this is the meaning

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

ūrdhvottaramṛṣibhyastu dhruvo yatra vyavasthitaḥ | etadviṣṇupadaṃ divyaṃ tṛtīyaṃ vyomni bhāsuram ||98||

divi ātataṃ sarva-prakāśaṃ sūrya-rūpaṃ cakṣur iva mahātmanāṃ vitataṃ sarva-bhāsakatvena pratataṃ viveka-jñānena prapañca-vailakṣaṇya-jñānena vṛddhaṃ ca aparicchinnatayā jñānam ity arthaḥ | anena ca tad viṣṇor iti mantrārthaḥ sūcitaḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

दिवि आततं सर्व-प्रकाशं सूर्य-रूपं चक्षुर् इव महात्मनां विततं सर्व-भासकत्वेन प्रततं विवेक-ज्ञानेन प्रपञ्च-वैलक्षण्य-ज्ञानेन वृद्धं च अपरिच्छिन्नतया ज्ञानम् इत्य् अर्थः । अनेन च तद् विष्णोर् इति मन्त्रार्थः सूचितः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains the divine eye as all-illuminating knowledge. It is compared to the Sun because it reveals everything, and it expands through discrimination between the self and the world.

VP 2.8.101

धर्मध्रुवाद्यास्तिष्ठन्ति यत्र ते लोकसाक्षिणः । तत्सार्ष्ट्योत्पन्नयोगर्धिस्तद्विष्णोः परमंपदम्

VP 2.8.102

यत्रोतमेतत्प्रोतं च यद्भूतं सचराचरम् । भव्यं य विश्वं मैत्रेय तद्विष्णोः परमं पदम्

What was there existing before, and what is going to come later on, created later; the three worlds, everything is controlled above the three worlds, are controlled like warp and weft of a cloth - the horizontal and vertical threads of a cloth. This is said to be Vishnu's Paramapada here.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yatrotametatprotaṃ ca yadbhūtaṃ sacarācaram — What was there existing before, and what is going to come later on, created later; bhavyaṃ ya viśvaṃ maitreya tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam — the three worlds, everything is controlled above the three worlds, are controlled like warp and weft of a cloth - the horizontal and vertical threads of a cloth. This is said to be Vishnu's Paramapada here.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yatrotametatprotaṃ ca yadbhūtaṃ sacarācaram | bhavyaṃ ya viśvaṃ maitreya tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam ||102||

Śrīdhara commentary.

No commentary.

VP 2.8.103

दिवीव चक्षुराततंयोगिनां तन्मयात्मनाम् । विवेकज्ञानदृष्टं च तद्विष्णोः परमं पदम्

All pervading, the chakshus is said to be Soorya here. They perceive through their knowledge, the Mahaatmaas there. As though the Sun in the Dyu loka, like this, their knowledge is spread all over. This is said to be Vishnu's Paramapada. This shloka is similar to the Shruti mantra - Diveeva chakshuraatatam ... sadaa pashyanti soorayah, which is told with respect to the Nityasooris, who are there in saakshaat Sri Vaikuntha itself. Yat vishnoh paramam padam sadaa pashyanti soorayah | Diveeva chakshuraatatam ...

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

divīva cakṣurātataṃyogināṃ tanmayātmanām — All pervading, the chakshus is said to be Soorya here. They perceive through their knowledge, the Mahaatmaas there. As though the Sun in the Dyu loka, like this, their knowledge is spread all over. This is said to be Vishnu's Paramapada. This shloka is similar to the Shruti mantra - Diveeva chakshuraatatam ... sadaa pashyanti soorayah, which is told with respect to the Nityasooris, who are there in saakshaat Sri Vaikuntha itself. Yat vishnoh paramam padam sadaa pashyanti soorayah | Diveeva chakshuraatatam ...; vivekajñānadṛṣṭaṃ ca tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam — All pervading, the chakshus is said to be Soorya here. They perceive through their knowledge, the Mahaatmaas there. As though the Sun in the Dyu loka, like this, their knowledge is spread all over. This is said to be Vishnu's Paramapada. This shloka is similar to the Shruti mantra - Diveeva chakshuraatatam ... sadaa pashyanti soorayah, which is told with respect to the Nityasooris, who are there in saakshaat Sri Vaikuntha itself. Yat vishnoh paramam padam sadaa pashyanti soorayah | Diveeva chakshuraatatam ...

VP 2.8.104

यस्मिन्प्रतिष्ठितो भास्वान्मेढीभूतः स्वयं ध्रुवः । ध्रुवे च सर्वज्योतींषि ज्योतिष्वम्भोमुचो द्विज

VP 2.8.105

मेघेषु संगता वृष्टिर्वृष्टेः सृष्टेश्च पोषणम् । आप्यायनं च सर्वेषां देवादीनां महामुने

Now, Paraasharar tells about River Gangaa. From there, the Gangaa river flows, which destroys all the sins. It has got a beautiful brownish hue, because of paste present in the bodies of the Apsaras and maidens there.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

megheṣu saṃgatā vṛṣṭirvṛṣṭeḥ sṛṣṭeśca poṣaṇam — Now; āpyāyanaṃ ca sarveṣāṃ devādīnāṃ mahāmune — Paraasharar tells about River Gangaa. From there, the Gangaa river flows, which destroys all the sins. It has got a beautiful brownish hue, because of paste present in the bodies of the Apsaras and maidens there.

Śrīdhara

vṛṣṭer hetoḥ — for the sake of rain; oṣadhi-dvārā sṛṣṭeḥ poṣaṇam — through plants, the nourishment of creation; vṛddhiḥ — growth; āpyāyanaṃ ca tṛptiḥ — and replenishment and satisfaction

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

megheṣu saṃgatā vṛṣṭirvṛṣṭeḥ sṛṣṭeśca poṣaṇam | āpyāyanaṃ ca sarveṣāṃ devādīnāṃ mahāmune ||105||

vṛṣṭerhetoḥ oṣadhidvārā sṛṣṭeḥ poṣaṇaṃ vṛddhiḥ āpyāyanañca tṛptiḥ

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

वृष्टेर्हेतोः ओषधिद्वारा सृष्टेः पोषणं वृद्धिः आप्यायनञ्च तृप्तिः

Śrīdhara commentary.

The commentary explains that rain nourishes creation through plants, producing growth and replenishment. This is how the waters support all beings.

vāryoghaiḥ saṃtatairyasyāḥ plāvitaṃ śaśimaṇḍalam | bhūyodhikatarāṃ kāntiṃ vahatyetaduha kṣaye ||111||

saptarṣayo yasya jale aghamarṣaṇaṃ kurvantastiṣṭhanti | uhyamānā itastatañcālyamānā jaṭā yeṣāṃ te

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

सप्तर्षयो यस्य जले अघमर्षणं कुर्वन्तस्तिष्ठन्ति । उह्यमाना इतस्ततञ्चाल्यमाना जटा येषां ते

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: saptarṣayo yasya jale aghamarṣaṇaṃ kurvantastiṣṭhanti | uhyamānā itastatañcālyamānā jaṭā yeṣāṃ te

VP 2.8.112

मेरुपृष्टे पतत्युच्चैर्निष्क्रान्ता शशिमण्डलात् । जगतः पावनार्थाय प्रयाति च चतुर्दिशम्

undesirous of progeny, and therefore victorious over death: eighty-eight thousand of these chaste beings tenant the regions of the sky,

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

merupṛṣṭe patatyuccairniṣkrāntā śaśimaṇḍalāt — undesirous of progeny, and therefore victorious over death: eighty-eight thousand of these chaste beings tenant the regions of the sky,; jagataḥ pāvanārthāya prayāti ca caturdiśam — undesirous of progeny, and therefore victorious over death: eighty-eight thousand of these chaste beings tenant the regions of the sky,

Śrīdhara

saptarṣayo yasya jale aghamarṣaṇaṃ kurvantastiṣṭhanti | uhyamānā itastatañcālyamānā jaṭā yeṣāṃ te — Śrīdhara gloss: saptarṣayo yasya jale aghamarṣaṇaṃ kurvantastiṣṭhanti | uhyamānā itastatañcālyamānā jaṭā yeṣāṃ te

Viṣṇu Purāṇa

merupṛṣṭe patatyuccairniṣkrāntā śaśimaṇḍalāt | jagataḥ pāvanārthāya prayāti ca caturdiśam ||112||

saptarṣayo yasya jale aghamarṣaṇaṃ kurvantastiṣṭhanti | uhyamānā itastatañcālyamānā jaṭā yeṣāṃ te

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

सप्तर्षयो यस्य जले अघमर्षणं कुर्वन्तस्तिष्ठन्ति । उह्यमाना इतस्ततञ्चाल्यमाना जटा येषां ते

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: saptarṣayo yasya jale aghamarṣaṇaṃ kurvantastiṣṭhanti | uhyamānā itastatañcālyamānā jaṭā yeṣāṃ te

VP 2.8.113

सीती चालकनन्दा च चक्षुर्भद्रा च संस्थिता । एकैव या चतुर्भेदा दिग्भेदगतिलक्षणा

north of the sun, until the destruction of the universe: they enjoy immortality, for that they are holy; exempt from covetousness and concupiscence, love

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

sītī cālakanandā ca cakṣurbhadrā ca saṃsthitā — north of the sun, until the destruction of the universe: they enjoy immortality, for that they are holy; ekaiva yā caturbhedā digbhedagatilakṣaṇā — exempt from covetousness and concupiscence, love

Śrīdhara

dikṣu catasṛṣu bhedena yā gatiḥ saiva caturbhedana lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā — Śrīdhara gloss: dikṣu catasṛṣu bhedena yā gatiḥ saiva caturbhedana lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

sītī cālakanandā ca cakṣurbhadrā ca saṃsthitā | ekaiva yā caturbhedā digbhedagatilakṣaṇā ||113||

dikṣu catasṛṣu bhedena yā gatiḥ saiva caturbhedana lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

दिक्षु चतसृषु भेदेन या गतिः सैव चतुर्भेदन लक्षणं यस्याः सा

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: dikṣu catasṛṣu bhedena yā gatiḥ saiva caturbhedana lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā

VP 2.8.114

भेदं चालकनन्दाख्यं यस्याः शर्वोपि दक्षिणाम् । दधार शिरसा प्रीत्य वर्षाणामधिकं शतम्

and hatred; taking no part in the procreation of living beings, and detecting the unreality of the

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

bhedaṃ cālakanandākhyaṃ yasyāḥ śarvopi dakṣiṇām — and hatred; dadhāra śirasā prītya varṣāṇāmadhikaṃ śatam — taking no part in the procreation of living beings, and detecting the unreality of the

Śrīdhara

dikṣu catasṛṣu bhedena yā gatiḥ saiva caturbhedana lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā — Śrīdhara gloss: dikṣu catasṛṣu bhedena yā gatiḥ saiva caturbhedana lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā

Viṣṇu Purāṇa

bhedaṃ cālakanandākhyaṃ yasyāḥ śarvopi dakṣiṇām | dadhāra śirasā prītya varṣāṇāmadhikaṃ śatam ||114||

dikṣu catasṛṣu bhedena yā gatiḥ saiva caturbhedana lakṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā

VP 2.8.115

शम्भोर्जटाकलापाच्च विनिष्क्रान्ताश्थिशर्कराः । प्लावयित्वा दिवं निन्ये या पापान्सगरात्मजान्

properties of elementary matter. By immortality is meant existence to the end of the Kalpa: life as long as

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

śambhorjaṭākalāpācca viniṣkrāntāśthiśarkarāḥ — properties of elementary matter. By immortality is meant existence to the end of the Kalpa: life as long as; plāvayitvā divaṃ ninye yā pāpānsagarātmajān — properties of elementary matter. By immortality is meant existence to the end of the Kalpa: life as long as

Śrīdhara

asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam — Śrīdhara gloss: asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

śambhorjaṭākalāpācca viniṣkrāntāśthiśarkarāḥ | plāvayitvā divaṃ ninye yā pāpānsagarātmajān ||115||

asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अस्थिशर्करा अस्थिचूर्णानि । पापाढ्यान् ब्रह्मदण्डहतानपि वर्णत्रयम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam

VP 2.8.116

स्त्रातस्य सलिले यस्याः सद्यः पापं प्रणश्यति । अपूर्वपुण्यप्राप्तिश्च सद्यो मैत्रेय जायते

If children give tarpana, with the waters of Gangaa, the forefathers, manes, attain trupti, satisfied for hundreds of years, which is impossible to attain.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

strātasya salile yasyāḥ sadyaḥ pāpaṃ praṇaśyati — If children give tarpana, with the waters of Gangaa, the forefathers, manes, attain trupti, satisfied for hundreds of years, which is impossible to attain.; apūrvapuṇyaprāptiśca sadyo maitreya jāyate — If children give tarpana, with the waters of Gangaa, the forefathers, manes, attain trupti, satisfied for hundreds of years, which is impossible to attain.

Śrīdhara

asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam — Śrīdhara gloss: asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

strātasya salile yasyāḥ sadyaḥ pāpaṃ praṇaśyati | apūrvapuṇyaprāptiśca sadyo maitreya jāyate ||116||

asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अस्थिशर्करा अस्थिचूर्णानि । पापाढ्यान् ब्रह्मदण्डहतानपि वर्णत्रयम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: asthiśarkarā asthicūrṇāni | pāpāḍhyān brahmadaṇḍahatānapi varṇatrayam

VP 2.8.117

दत्ताः पितृभ्यो यत्रापस्तनयैः श्रद्धयान्वितैः । समाशतं प्रयच्छन्ति तृप्तिं मैत्रेय दुर्लभाम्

iniquity or piety, such as Brahmanicide or an Aśwamedha, endure for a similar period, or until

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

dattāḥ pitṛbhyo yatrāpastanayaiḥ śraddhayānvitaiḥ — iniquity or piety, such as Brahmanicide or an Aśwamedha, endure for a similar period, or until; samāśataṃ prayacchanti tṛptiṃ maitreya durlabhām — iniquity or piety, such as Brahmanicide or an Aśwamedha, endure for a similar period, or until

Śrīdhara

samātrayaṃ varṣatrayam — Śrīdhara gloss: samātrayaṃ varṣatrayam

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

dattāḥ pitṛbhyo yatrāpastanayaiḥ śraddhayānvitaiḥ | samāśataṃ prayacchanti tṛptiṃ maitreya durlabhām ||117||

samātrayaṃ varṣatrayam

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

समात्रयं वर्षत्रयम्

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: samātrayaṃ varṣatrayam

VP 2.8.118

यस्यामिष्ट्वा महायज्ञैर्यज्ञेशं पुरुषोत्तमम् । द्विजभूपाः परां सिद्धिमवापुर्दिवि चेह च

Using the waters of Gangaa, if they do mahaa yajnyaas, and worship Purushottama, the One who is aaraadhya by these yajnyaas, Yajnyesha. brahmins obtain great benefits and purushaarthaas, in this as well as other worlds. This is telling how sacred and holy the Gangaa waters are. One who does tarpana, does yajnya, takes bath, all attain great benefit.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yasyāmiṣṭvā mahāyajñairyajñeśaṃ puruṣottamam — Using the waters of Gangaa, if they do mahaa yajnyaas, and worship Purushottama, the One who is aaraadhya by these yajnyaas; dvijabhūpāḥ parāṃ siddhimavāpurdivi ceha ca — Yajnyesha. brahmins obtain great benefits and purushaarthaas, in this as well as other worlds. This is telling how sacred and holy the Gangaa waters are. One who does tarpana, does yajnya, takes bath, all attain great benefit.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yasyāmiṣṭvā mahāyajñairyajñeśaṃ puruṣottamam | dvijabhūpāḥ parāṃ siddhimavāpurdivi ceha ca ||118||

Śrīdhara commentary.

No commentary.

VP 2.8.119

स्नानाद्विधूतपापाश्च यज्जलैर्यतयस्तथा । केशबासक्तमनसः प्राप्ता निर्वाणमुत्तमम्

One who takes bath gets rid of all the sins. The sages took bath there and got rid of all sins. They were meditating on Keshava, attained Nirvaana, great aananda.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

snānādvidhūtapāpāśca yajjalairyatayastathā — One who takes bath gets rid of all the sins. The sages took bath there and got rid of all sins. They were meditating on Keshava, attained Nirvaana, great aananda.; keśabāsaktamanasaḥ prāptā nirvāṇamuttamam — One who takes bath gets rid of all the sins. The sages took bath there and got rid of all sins. They were meditating on Keshava, attained Nirvaana, great aananda.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

snānādvidhūtapāpāśca yajjalairyatayastathā | keśabāsaktamanasaḥ prāptā nirvāṇamuttamam ||119||

Śrīdhara commentary.

No commentary.

VP 2.8.120

श्रुताभिलषिता दृष्टा स्पृष्टा पीतावगाहिता । या पावयति भूतानि कीर्तिता च दिवेदिने

Whether one hears about the greatness of the river Gangaa, or one gets a desire to take bath in Gangaa river, or one who sees the Gangaa itself, or one who touches the waters of Gangaa, one who drinks the waters of sacred Gangaa, one who does avagaaha snaana in it, one who even praises the great qualities and powers of the river Gangaa, all of them get purified, and sins get destroyed.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

śrutābhilaṣitā dṛṣṭā spṛṣṭā pītāvagāhitā — Whether one hears about the greatness of the river Gangaa, or one gets a desire to take bath in Gangaa river, or one who sees the Gangaa itself, or one who touches the waters of Gangaa, one who drinks the waters of sacred Gangaa, one who does avagaaha snaana in it, one who even praises the great qualities and powers of the river Gangaa, all of them get purified, and sins get destroyed.; yā pāvayati bhūtāni kīrtitā ca divedine — Whether one hears about the greatness of the river Gangaa, or one gets a desire to take bath in Gangaa river, or one who sees the Gangaa itself, or one who touches the waters of Gangaa, one who drinks the waters of sacred Gangaa, one who does avagaaha snaana in it, one who even praises the great qualities and powers of the river Gangaa, all of them get purified, and sins get destroyed.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

śrutābhilaṣitā dṛṣṭā spṛṣṭā pītāvagāhitā | yā pāvayati bhūtāni kīrtitā ca divedine ||120||

Śrīdhara commentary.

No commentary.

VP 2.8.121

गगागङ्गेति यैर्नामयोजनाना शतष्वपि । स्थितैरुच्चारितं हन्ति पापं जन्मत्रयार्जितम्

Those who are even hundred yojanaas away from Gangaa river, if they tell thrice as " Gangaa Gangaa Gangaa", they will be freed from sins accumulated from three janmaas.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

gagāgaṅgeti yairnāmayojanānā śataṣvapi — Those who are even hundred yojanaas away from Gangaa river, if they tell thrice as " Gangaa Gangaa Gangaa", they will be freed from sins accumulated from three janmaas.; sthitairuccāritaṃ hanti pāpaṃ janmatrayārjitam — Those who are even hundred yojanaas away from Gangaa river, if they tell thrice as " Gangaa Gangaa Gangaa", they will be freed from sins accumulated from three janmaas.

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

gagāgaṅgeti yairnāmayojanānā śataṣvapi | sthitairuccāritaṃ hanti pāpaṃ janmatrayārjitam ||121||

Śrīdhara commentary.

No commentary.

VP 2.8.122

यतः सा पावनायालं त्रयाणां जगतामपि । समुद्भूता परं तत्तु तृतीयं भगवत्पदम्

are extinct, in consequence of the cessation of the consequences of piety or iniquity, and where they never sorrow more.

Word-for-word
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yataḥ sā pāvanāyālaṃ trayāṇāṃ jagatāmapi — are extinct, in consequence of the cessation of the consequences of piety or iniquity, and where they never sorrow more.; samudbhūtā paraṃ tattu tṛtīyaṃ bhagavatpadam — are extinct, in consequence of the cessation of the consequences of piety or iniquity, and where they never sorrow more.

Śrīdhara

prastutaṃ viṣṇupadaṃ — the Viṣṇu-foot just discussed; nigamayati — he concludes; yata iti — “from which,” thus; yataḥ sthānāt — from which place; samudbhatā prākaṭyaṃ prāptā — arisen and attained manifestation; tat bhagavatpadam iti — that is the Lord’s foot

Transliteration
Viṣṇu Purāṇa

yataḥ sā pāvanāyālaṃ trayāṇāṃ jagatāmapi | samudbhūtā paraṃ tattu tṛtīyaṃ bhagavatpadam ||122||

prastutaṃ viṣṇupadaṃ nigamayati—yata iti | yataḥ sthānāt samudbhatā prākaṭyaṃ prāptā, tat bhagavatpadam iti

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

प्रस्तुतं विष्णुपदं निगमयति—यत इति । यतः स्थानात् समुद्भता प्राकट्यं प्राप्ता, तत् भगवत्पदम् इति

Śrīdhara commentary.

Śrīdhara gloss: prastutaṃ viṣṇupadaṃ nigamayati—yata iti | yataḥ sthānāt samudbhatā prākaṭyaṃ prāptā, tat bhagavatpadam iti
